<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:main-prompt-generating-process}
Each prompt and its target are generated as follows.
\begin{enumerate}[labelindent=0pt,leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,parsep=1pt,topsep=0pt,partopsep=0pt]
  \item Sample a latent task $\Theta\sim\Pi$.

  \item Conditional on $\Theta$, generate $n+1$ complete examples independently from the task-specific distribution:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item For \textbf{sample-only prompts}, a task $\theta$ specifies a distribution $P_\theta$ on ${\mathcal{Z}}$, where $\theta\mapsto P_\theta$ is a Borel probability kernel, and $Z_1,\ldots,Z_n,Z_\star \stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P_\Theta$.
    \item For \textbf{input--output prompts}, fix an input dimension $d_x\ge1$ and let ${\mathcal{X}}=[0,1]^{d_x}$.
    A task $\theta$ specifies a joint distribution $P_\theta^{X,Z}$ on ${\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Z}}$, where $\theta\mapsto P_\theta^{X,Z}$ is a Borel probability kernel, and $(X_1,Z_1),\ldots,(X_n,Z_n),(X_\star,Z_\star) \stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}P_\Theta^{X,Z}$.
  \end{itemize}

  \item Form the context from the first $n$ examples and hold out the output in the final example as the target: $C_n=Z_{1:n}, H_n=C_n$ for sample-only prompts and $C_n=((X_i,Z_i))_{i=1}^n, H_n=(C_n,X_\star)$ for input--output prompts.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{assump:smooth-latent-task}
  Tasks are indexed by a parameter $\theta\in{\mathcal{T}}=[0,1]^{d_{\theta}}$, with $d_{\theta}\in\mathbb{N}$ fixed across the model class and independent of $n$, but the sampled $\Theta$ remains latent and $\Pi$ may be any Borel probability measure on ${\mathcal{T}}$.
  Let $g_\theta$ denote the Lebesgue density of $P_\theta$ or $P_\theta^{X,Z}$ in the sample-only or input--output model, respectively. 
  Fix $\beta>0$, $L_{\beta}>1$, $\underline{p}\in(0,1]$, $\beta_{\theta}\in(0,1]$, and $H_{\theta}\in[0,\infty)$.
  The map $(\theta,u)\mapsto g_{\theta}(u)$ is jointly Borel and, for all $\theta,\theta'\in{\mathcal{T}}$,
  \begin{equation*}
    g_{\theta}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta,+}^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}(L_{\beta},\underline{p}),
    \qquad
    \sup_{u\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}}|g_{\theta}(u)-g_{\theta'}(u)|
    \le H_{\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}.
                                          
  \end{equation*}
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main-generation-error}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, for each $n\ge2$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, there exists a common DiT class and a choice of diffusion parameters with model size and forward-pass cost polynomial in $(n,\varepsilon^{-1})$. For every fixed $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ and $\delta\in(0,1)$, pretraining on $N$ independent episodes to empirical optimization gap $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}\lesssim\varepsilon^2$ and sampling with $M$ admissible reverse steps, as in Section~\ref{subsec:main-training-and-sampling}, yield, with probability at least $1-\delta$ over the training episodes and their diffusion augmentations,
\begin{equation}
{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat P^{(M)}_{0\mid H_n},P(\cdot\mid H_n)\bigr)
\lesssim
\varepsilon
+ \log(e/\varepsilon)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}\log(en/\varepsilon)+\log(2/\delta)}{N}}
+ \frac{\log(e/\varepsilon)}{\sqrt M},
\label{eq:main-predictive-generation-bound}
\end{equation}
where $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ is the number of trainable parameters.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:informal-task-uncertainty}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, the following statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Uniform upper bound.}
  Every $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ and every $n\ge2$ satisfy
  \begin{equation*}
    {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}\operatorname{TV}\!\left(P(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\right)
    \lesssim \sqrt{\log n/n}.
  \end{equation*}
  \item \textbf{Matching worst-case lower bound.}
  If, in addition, $H_\theta>0$, $\underline{p}<1$, and $L_\beta>1$, then for every sufficiently large $n$, there is a model $\mathcal{M}_n\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ for which
  \begin{equation*}
    \inf_{Q^{\mathcal{M}_n}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}_n}\operatorname{TV}\!\left(Q^{\mathcal{M}_n}(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\right)
    \gtrsim \sqrt{\log n/n},
  \end{equation*}
  where the infimum is over prompt-only Borel probability kernels from ${\mathcal{H}}_n$ to ${\mathcal{Z}}$ that may know $\mathcal{M}_n$.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:informal-task-generation}
For sufficiently large $n$, set $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log n/n}$, use the score class and estimator of \cref{thm:main-generation-error} with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}\lesssim\log n/n$, and let $N$ and $M$ satisfy \cref{eq:main-predictive-sufficient-choices} at this value of $\varepsilon$. For every fixed $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ and $\delta\in(0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$ over pretraining and its diffusion augmentations,
\begin{equation}
  {\mathbb{E}}\operatorname{TV}\!\left(\widehat P^{(M)}_{0\mid H_n},P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\right)
  \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.
  \label{eq:informal-task-generation}
\end{equation}
Here the expectation is over a fresh test episode conditional on the completed training run.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:balanced-dsm-identity}
Define $\widetilde{s}^{\star}(z,t\,|\, h):=\sigma_t s^{\star}(z,t\,|\, h)$.
Then
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}[-\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}} \,|\,
  Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n]
  = \widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_{\tau},
  \tau\,|\, H_n)
\end{equation*}
almost surely, and every jointly Borel score $s$ satisfying ${\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{bDSM}}(s)<\infty$ obeys
\begin{equation}
  {\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{bDSM}}(s)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{bDSM}}(s^{\star})
  = {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(s).
  \label{eq:balanced-dsm-excess-identity}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The standard denoising identity \citep{vincent2011}, multiplied by $\sigma_t$, gives the conditional mean at each fixed $t>0$.
Joint measurability and disintegration under $\varpi_{t_0,T}$ give the asserted identity at $\tau$.
Conditional Jensen and $\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}}\in L^2$ imply $\widetilde{s}^{\star}\in L^2$ and finite risk.
For every finite-risk $s$, the squared-loss Pythagorean identity therefore yields
\begin{align*}
  {\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{bDSM}}(s)-{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{bDSM}}(s^{\star})
  &=A_{t_0,T}{\mathbb{E}}\|\widetilde{s}(Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n)
       -\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_{\tau},\tau\mid H_n)\|_2^2\\
  &={\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(s),
\end{align*}
where the second equality uses $A_{t_0,T}\varpi_{t_0,T}(t)\sigma_t^2=1$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:balanced-score-truncation}
For the cube-supported posterior-predictive distributions in \cref{app:subsec:probability-conventions}, $\|\widetilde{s}^{\star}(z,t\mid h)\|_2\le R_{\mathrm{bal},n}$ on $\mathcal{G}_n$, and
\begin{equation}
  \delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
  \le \Pr\!\left(\|\xi\|_2>R_{\mathrm{state},n}-\sqrt{d_z}\right)<1,
  \qquad \xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d_z}).
  \label{eq:transformer-good-event-probability}
\end{equation}
The squared-score contribution outside the truncation region is finite and satisfies
\begin{align}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2
  &:=A_{t_0,T}{\mathbb{E}}\!\left[
  \|\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_{\tau},\tau\mid H_n)\|_2^2
  \operatorname{\mathds{1}}\{(H_n,Z_{\tau},\tau)\notin\mathcal{G}_n\}
  \right]
  \label{eq:transformer-exceptional-score-energy}\\
  &\le \frac{A_{t_0,T}}{\sigma_{t_0}^2}
  {\mathbb{E}}\!\left[
  (\|\xi\|_2+2\sqrt{d_z})^2
  \operatorname{\mathds{1}}\{\|\xi\|_2>R_{\mathrm{state},n}-\sqrt{d_z}\}
  \right].
  \label{eq:derived-score-tail-bound}
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
By \cref{eq:positive-time-score-regularity} and cube support,
\begin{equation*}
  \|\widetilde{s}^{\star}(z,t\mid h)\|_2
  \le\frac{\|z\|_2+\sqrt{d_z}}{\sigma_{t_0}},
  \qquad t\in[t_0,T].
\end{equation*}
This proves the uniform bound on $\mathcal{G}_n$.
Since $\|Z_0\|_2\le\sqrt{d_z}$ and $\alpha_{\tau},\sigma_{\tau}\le1$, forward noising gives
\begin{equation*}
  \{\|Z_{\tau}\|_2>R_{\mathrm{state},n}\}
  \subseteq
  \{\|\xi\|_2>R_{\mathrm{state},n}-\sqrt{d_z}\},
  \qquad
  \|Z_{\tau}\|_2+\sqrt{d_z}\le\|\xi\|_2+2\sqrt{d_z}.
\end{equation*}
The Gaussian probability proves \cref{eq:transformer-good-event-probability}; its strict bound follows from $R_{\mathrm{state},n}>\sqrt{d_z}$.
Combining the score bound with these inequalities and applying Tonelli proves \cref{eq:derived-score-tail-bound}.
Joint Borel measurability follows from the chosen score version and the fixed clipping set, and Gaussian second moments give finiteness.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:positive-smooth-latent-derived-regularity}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, the preceding maps are jointly Borel, and $q_{\theta}(\cdot\,|\, x)$ is a probability density for every $(\theta,x)$.
There are fixed constants $B_{\ell,\mathrm{ctx}}\ge1$, $B_{\ell,\mathrm{qry}}\ge0$, $0<\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}\le\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}$, and finite H\"older constants such that, for every $\theta,\theta'\in{\mathcal{T}}$, every $c,c'\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}$, every $x,x'\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\star}$, and every $z_0\in[0,1]^{d_z}$,
\begin{equation}
  |\ell_{\theta}(c)|\le B_{\ell,\mathrm{ctx}},
  \qquad
  |\ell_{\theta}^{\mathrm{qry}}(x)|\le B_{\ell,\mathrm{qry}},
  \qquad
  \underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}\le q_{\theta}(z_0\,|\, x)\le\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-derived-envelope}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{align}
  |\ell_{\theta}(c)-\ell_{\theta'}(c')|
  &\le
  H_{\ell,\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}
  {}+
  H_{\ell,c}\|c-c'\|_2^{\beta'},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-context-regularity}\\
  |\ell_{\theta}^{\mathrm{qry}}(x)
  -\ell_{\theta'}^{\mathrm{qry}}(x')|
  &\le
  H_{\mathrm{qry},\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}
  {}+
  H_{\mathrm{qry},x}\|x-x'\|_2^{\beta'},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-query-regularity}\\
  \sup_{z_0\in[0,1]^{d_z}}
  |q_{\theta}(z_0\,|\, x)-q_{\theta'}(z_0\,|\, x')|
  &\le
  H_{q,\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}
  {}+
  H_{q,x}\|x-x'\|_2^{\beta'}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-output-regularity}
\end{align}
In the sample-only prompt model,
\begin{equation*}
  (B_{\ell,\mathrm{qry}},H_{\mathrm{qry},\theta},
  H_{\mathrm{qry},x},H_{q,x})=(0,0,0,0).
\end{equation*}
The constants depend only on the parameters in \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}; explicit choices for the sample-only and input--output prompt models are given in \cref{eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants,eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants-sample}.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The defining H\"older bound for $\beta\le1$, and the mean-value theorem with the H\"older norm's gradient bound for $\beta>1$, give a constant
\begin{equation*}
  H_{\mathrm{dens}}:=C_{\beta,d_{\mathrm{stat}}}L_{\beta}
\end{equation*}
such that every $g_{\theta}$ in \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} satisfies
\begin{equation*}
  |g_{\theta}(u)-g_{\theta}(u')|
  \le H_{\mathrm{dens}}\|u-u'\|_2^{\beta'}.
\end{equation*}
Put
\begin{equation*}
  B_{\log}:=1\vee|\log\underline{p}|\vee\log L_{\beta},
  \qquad
  K_q:=\underline{p}^{-1}+L_{\beta}\underline{p}^{-2}.
\end{equation*}
In the input--output prompt model, one may choose
\begin{align}
  B_{\ell,\mathrm{ctx}}=B_{\ell,\mathrm{qry}}&:=B_{\log},
  &
  (\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}})
  &:=\left(\frac{\underline{p}}{L_{\beta}},
  \frac{L_{\beta}}{\underline{p}}\right),
  \nonumber\\
  H_{\ell,\theta}=H_{\mathrm{qry},\theta}
  &:=\frac{H_{\theta}}{\underline{p}},
  &
  H_{\ell,c}=H_{\mathrm{qry},x}
  &:=\frac{H_{\mathrm{dens}}}{\underline{p}},
  \nonumber\\
  H_{q,\theta}&:=K_qH_{\theta},
  &
  H_{q,x}&:=K_qH_{\mathrm{dens}}.
  \label{eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants}
\end{align}
In the sample-only prompt model, one may instead choose
\begin{equation}
  \begin{gathered}
  B_{\ell,\mathrm{ctx}}:=B_{\log},
  \qquad
  (\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}):=(\underline{p},L_{\beta}),
  \qquad
  H_{\ell,\theta}:=H_{\theta}/\underline{p},
  \qquad
  H_{\ell,c}:=H_{\mathrm{dens}}/\underline{p},
  \\
  H_{q,\theta}:=H_{\theta},
  \qquad
  (B_{\ell,\mathrm{qry}},H_{\mathrm{qry},\theta},
  H_{\mathrm{qry},x},H_{q,x}):=(0,0,0,0).
  \end{gathered}
  \label{eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants-sample}
\end{equation}

We verify the input--output case.
Because the output cube has unit volume,
\begin{equation*}
  \underline{p}\le\lambda_{\theta}(x)\le L_{\beta}
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
  |\lambda_{\theta}(x)-\lambda_{\theta'}(x')|
  \le
  H_{\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}
  +H_{\mathrm{dens}}\|x-x'\|_2^{\beta'}.
\end{equation*}
The mean-value theorem for the logarithm on $[\underline{p},L_{\beta}]$ proves the asserted bounds for $\ell_{\theta}$ and $\ell_{\theta}^{\mathrm{qry}}$.
The bounds on $g_{\theta}$ and $\lambda_{\theta}$ give the displayed values of $\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}$ and $\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}$.
Moreover, for $u=(x,z_0)$ and $u'=(x',z_0)$,
\begin{align*}
  |q_{\theta}(z_0\mid x)-q_{\theta'}(z_0\mid x')|
  &\le
  \frac{|g_{\theta}(u)-g_{\theta'}(u')|}{\underline{p}}
  +\frac{L_{\beta}
  |\lambda_{\theta}(x)-\lambda_{\theta'}(x')|}{\underline{p}^2}
  \\
  &\le
  K_q\left\{
  H_{\theta}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_{\theta}}
  +H_{\mathrm{dens}}\|x-x'\|_2^{\beta'}
  \right\}.
\end{align*}
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-derived-envelope,eq:smooth-latent-context-regularity,eq:smooth-latent-query-regularity,eq:smooth-latent-output-regularity} with \cref{eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants}.
The sample-only case follows directly from $q_{\theta}=g_{\theta}$, the density bounds, the latent modulus, and the mean-value bound for the logarithm, giving \cref{eq:positive-smooth-latent-derived-constants-sample}.
Joint Borel measurability follows from parameterized integration and composition with continuous functions on intervals bounded away from zero.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-normalized-basis-regularity}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, put $\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}:=[-1,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}+1}$
and $R_{\mathrm{geom},n}:=1+R_{\mathrm{state},n}+\sqrt{d_z}$.
For every $\theta\in{\mathcal{T}}$, there exist continuous maps
\begin{equation*}
  \overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}:\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}
  \to[\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}],
  \qquad
  \overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}:\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}
  \to\overline{\operatorname{Ball}}(0,R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}),
\end{equation*}
such that, for every $(h,z,t)\in\mathcal{G}_n$,
\begin{equation*}
  \overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}(\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h))
  =\mathsf{a}_{\theta}(x_{\star},z,t),
  \qquad
  \overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}(\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h))
  =\mathsf{b}_{\theta}(x_{\star},z,t).
\end{equation*}
Moreover, there is a single finite constant $H_{\mathrm{bas},n}$, independent of $\theta$, such that, for all $u,u'\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}$,
\begin{equation*}
  |\overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}(u)-\overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}(u')|
  +\|\overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}(u)-\overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}(u')\|_2
  \le H_{\mathrm{bas},n}\|u-u'\|_2^{\beta'}.
\end{equation*}
The common H\"older constant satisfies
\begin{equation}
  \begin{aligned}
  H_{\mathrm{bas},n}\le{} C_{\mathrm{bas}}\big[&
  1+(1+R_{\mathrm{bal},n})H_{q,x}
  \\
  &+R_{\mathrm{state},n}
  \{R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{t_0}^{-2}
  +\sigma_{t_0}^{-1}
  +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{t_0}^{-3}\}
  \\
  &+T\{R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{t_0}^{-4}
  +\sigma_{t_0}^{-1}
  +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{t_0}^{-3}
  +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^3\sigma_{t_0}^{-5}\}
  \big].
  \end{aligned}
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-basis-regularity}
\end{equation}
Here $0<C_{\mathrm{bas}}<\infty$ depends only on the fixed dimensions, $\beta'$, and the common density bounds.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The Gaussian identities
\begin{align*}
  \nabla_z\Lambda_t(z,y)
  &=-\sigma_t^{-1}I_{d_z},\\
  \nabla_z\log G_t(z,y)
  &=\sigma_t^{-1}\Lambda_t(z,y),\\
  \partial_t\Lambda_t(z,y)
  &=-\frac{\alpha_ty}{\sigma_t}
  -\frac{\alpha_t^2}{\sigma_t^2}\Lambda_t(z,y)
  ,\\
  \partial_t\log G_t(z,y)
  &=-\frac{d_z\alpha_t^2}{\sigma_t^2}
  -\frac{(z-\alpha_ty)^{\top}\alpha_ty}{\sigma_t^2}
  +\frac{\alpha_t^2\|z-\alpha_ty\|_2^2}{\sigma_t^4}
\end{align*}
and the covariance identity below imply the derivative bounds
\begin{align*}
  \|\nabla_z\mathsf{a}_{\theta}\|_2
  &\lesssim R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{t_0}^{-2},
  &
  \|\nabla_z\mathsf{b}_{\theta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
  &\lesssim \sigma_{t_0}^{-1}+R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{t_0}^{-3},
  \\
  |\partial_t\mathsf{a}_{\theta}|
  &\lesssim R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{t_0}^{-4},
  &
  \|\partial_t\mathsf{b}_{\theta}\|_2
  &\lesssim \sigma_{t_0}^{-1}+R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{t_0}^{-3}
  +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^3\sigma_{t_0}^{-5}.
\end{align*}
The implicit constants depend only on the fixed dimensions and density bounds.
The denominator $I_t(z)$ causes no extra small-denominator factor because, for every bounded differentiable scalar- or vector-valued integrand $F$ and each coordinate derivative
\begin{equation*}
  D\in\{\partial_t,\partial_{z_1},\ldots,\partial_{z_{d_z}}\},
\end{equation*}
one has, componentwise in the vector-valued case,
\begin{equation}
  D{\mathbb{E}}_{\nu_{z,t}}[F(z,t,Y)]
  ={\mathbb{E}}_{\nu_{z,t}}[D F(z,t,Y)]
  +\operatorname{Cov}_{\nu_{z,t}}
  \bigl(F(z,t,Y),D\log G_t(z,Y)\bigr).
  \label{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-covariance-identity}
\end{equation}
Differentiation is justified by domination on the compact domain for $(x,z,t)$ defined above.
For the query coordinate, \cref{eq:smooth-latent-output-regularity} and \cref{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-normalized-bases} give a $\beta'$-H\"older modulus at most
\begin{equation*}
  (1+R_{\mathrm{bal},n})H_{q,x}.
\end{equation*}
To define the functions approximated by the network on the entire normalized cube, let $u\in\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}$, written as $u=(u_x,u_z,u_t)$ in the input--output prompt model and $u=(u_z,u_t)$ in the sample-only prompt model, and set
\begin{equation*}
  \begin{aligned}
  x(u)&:=\begin{cases}
  0,&\text{sample-only},\\
  (u_x+\mathbf{1}_{d_x})/2,&\text{input--output},
  \end{cases}\\
  z(u)&:=R_{\mathrm{state},n}{\mathrm{clip}}_1(u_z),
  &
  t(u)&:=t_0+\frac{T-t_0}{2}(u_t+1).
  \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Define the extensions by
\begin{equation*}
  \overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}(u)
  :=\mathsf{a}_{\theta}(x(u),z(u),t(u)),
  \qquad
  \overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}(u)
  :=\mathsf{b}_{\theta}(x(u),z(u),t(u)).
\end{equation*}
Since ${\mathrm{clip}}_1$ is one-Lipschitz, the three coordinate maps above have Lipschitz constants bounded by a fixed constant, $R_{\mathrm{state},n}$, and $T$, respectively.
Multiplying the state and time derivative bounds by the Lipschitz constants of these coordinate maps and adding the query modulus gives the asserted H\"older inequality, with $H_{\mathrm{bas},n}$ satisfying \cref{eq:smooth-latent-basis-regularity}, after increasing $C_{\mathrm{bas}}$ by a fixed dimension-dependent factor.
By construction, the extensions take values in the ranges stated in the lemma.
For every $(h,z,t)\in\mathcal{G}_n$, the state block of $\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h)$ lies in the unit ball and represents the unclipped state; substituting the feature coordinates into the definitions above therefore gives $\overline{\mathsf{a}}_{\theta,n}(\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h))=\mathsf{a}_{\theta}(x_{\star},z,t)$ and $\overline{\mathsf{b}}_{\theta,n}(\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h))=\mathsf{b}_{\theta}(x_{\star},z,t)$.
Since $\mathcal{U}_{\mathrm{feat}}$ has bounded diameter and $\beta'\le1$, the state--time Lipschitz bounds are also $\beta'$-H\"older after increasing their constant.
This proves all claims of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:episode-kl-score-approximation}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, fix an integer $J\ge1$, pairwise distinct grid points $\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_J\in{\mathcal{T}}$, and $\eta\ge0$, and let $\pi_{\eta}:{\mathcal{T}}\to\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_J\}$ be Borel with $\|\theta-\pi_{\eta}(\theta)\|_2\le\eta$ for every $\theta$.
Set $w_j=\Pr\!\bigl(\pi_{\eta}(\Theta)=\theta_j\bigr)$ and, for $\zeta\in[0,1]$, set
\begin{equation}
 w_j^{\zeta}:=\frac{w_j+\zeta/J}{1+\zeta}.
 \label{eq:integrated-prior-weights}
\end{equation}
Let $Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{\mathrm{epi}}$ be the observed-episode distribution obtained by drawing a grid task with weights $w_j^{\zeta}$ and then drawing $n+1$ conditionally independent examples from $g_{\theta_j}$.
In the input--output model, an example is a full pair $(X,Z)$ and the observed episode is $(C_n,X_\star,Z_\star)$.
Because every grid density is positive, let $Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{0\mid h}$ denote the everywhere-defined Bayes conditional law of $Z_\star$ given $H_n=h$ under $Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{\mathrm{epi}}$.
If $q_t^{\eta,\zeta}(\cdot\mid h)$ denotes its OU-smoothed density, define
$s^{\eta,\zeta}(z,t\mid h):=\nabla_z\log q_t^{\eta,\zeta}(z\mid h)$.
Define $D_n(\eta,\zeta)=(n+1)H_\theta^2\eta^{2\beta_\theta}/\underline{p}+\log(1+\zeta)$.
Then
\begin{align}
 \mathrm{KL}\!\left({\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{epi}}^{(n)}\middle\|Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{\mathrm{epi}}\right)
 &\le D_n(\eta,\zeta), \label{eq:integrated-episode-kl}\\
 {\mathbb{E}}_{H_n}\mathrm{KL}(P_{0\mid H_n}\|Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{0\mid H_n})
 &\le D_n(\eta,\zeta). \label{eq:integrated-predictive-kl}
\end{align}
Moreover, for every $0<t_0<T<\infty$,
\begin{equation}
 \int_{t_0}^T{\mathbb{E}}\left[
 \|s^\star(Z_t,t\mid H_n)-s^{\eta,\zeta}(Z_t,t\mid H_n)\|_2^2\right]\,{\mathrm{d}} t
 \le D_n(\eta,\zeta), \label{eq:integrated-latent-score-bound}
\end{equation}
where $(H_n,Z_t)$ has the original prompt and conditional diffusion law.
The result is uniform over Borel task priors, including priors with zero cell masses.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
For densities $g,g'$ on a unit-volume cube with $g'\ge\underline{p}$, $\log u\le u-1$ gives
\begin{equation*}
 \mathrm{KL}(g\|g')\le\int\frac{(g-g')^2}{g'}
 \le\underline{p}^{-1}\|g-g'\|_\infty^2.
\end{equation*}
Set $U_i:=Z_i$ in the sample-only model and $U_i:=(X_i,Z_i)$ in the input--output model. Compare the two joint laws of $(\Theta,U_1,\ldots,U_{n+1})$ with the same prior $\Pi$, using $g_\Theta$ and $g_{\pi_\eta(\Theta)}$ as the conditional example densities.
Tensorization of conditional KL gives a bound $(n+1)H_\theta^2\eta^{2\beta_\theta}/\underline{p}$.
Marginalizing $\Theta$ gives the same bound for the full example sequence.
Reordering that sequence into $(H_n,Z_\star)$ is a measurable bijection under either the sample-only or the input--output prompt model, so it preserves KL.
This proves \cref{eq:integrated-episode-kl} for $\zeta=0$.

Let $Q^{\eta,\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{epi}}$ use the uniform distribution over the same grid.
The weights in \cref{eq:integrated-prior-weights} give the identity
\begin{equation*}
 Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{\mathrm{epi}}
 =\frac{Q^{\eta,0}_{\mathrm{epi}}+\zeta Q^{\eta,\mathrm{unif}}_{\mathrm{epi}}}{1+\zeta}.
\end{equation*}
Its density is at least $(1+\zeta)^{-1}$ times the density of $Q^{\eta,0}_{\mathrm{epi}}$.
Integrating the resulting log-density inequality proves \cref{eq:integrated-episode-kl}.
The KL chain rule at $(H_n,Z_\star)$ proves \cref{eq:integrated-predictive-kl}: the conditional KL is averaged under the original prompt law, and the chain rule also contains a nonnegative KL term for the prompt marginal that can be dropped.

Fix $h$ in a $\mathsf{Law}(H_n)$-full-measure set on which the conditional laws are defined, and write $p_t,q_t$ for the OU-smoothed densities of $P_{0\mid h},Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{0\mid h}$.
Their initial densities on the output cube lie in $[\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}]$ by \cref{lem:positive-smooth-latent-derived-regularity}.
Hence their ratio after convolution is bounded above and below by positive constants.
For $t\in[t_0,T]$, convolution with the OU kernel gives smooth positive densities with Gaussian tails.
Their spatial derivatives are bounded by Gaussian envelopes times polynomials in $\|z\|$, uniformly on this time interval.
In particular, the score identity from differentiating the kernel gives $\|\nabla\log p_t(z)\|+\|\nabla\log q_t(z)\|\lesssim_{t_0,T,d_z}1+\|z\|$.

Both densities solve $\partial_t p=\Delta p+\nabla\cdot(zp)$.
Differentiating $\int p_t\log(p_t/q_t)$ and integrating by parts therefore gives the relative-entropy dissipation identity
\begin{equation}
 \frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}} t}\mathrm{KL}(p_t\|q_t)
 =-\int p_t\|\nabla\log p_t-\nabla\log q_t\|_2^2\,{\mathrm{d}} z.
 \label{eq:ou-relative-entropy-dissipation}
\end{equation}
The drift terms cancel and the coefficient is one for diffusion coefficient $\sqrt{2}$; compare \citet{chengeorgioupavon2016}.
To justify the calculation on ${\mathbb{R}}^{d_z}$, insert smooth cutoffs equal to one on a ball of radius $R$, with first and second derivatives of order $R^{-1}$ and $R^{-2}$, respectively, perform the calculation on their compact support, and let $R\to\infty$.
The bounded density ratio, the score growth bound, and the Gaussian bounds on the derivatives give an integrable upper bound for every differentiated and boundary term, so dominated convergence applies.
Integrating \cref{eq:ou-relative-entropy-dissipation} gives
\begin{align*}
 \int_{t_0}^T\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d_z}} p_t(z)
 \|s^\star(z,t\mid h)-s^{\eta,\zeta}(z,t\mid h)\|_2^2\,{\mathrm{d}} z\,{\mathrm{d}} t
 &=\mathrm{KL}(p_{t_0}\|q_{t_0})-\mathrm{KL}(p_T\|q_T)\\
 &\le\mathrm{KL}(P_{0\mid h}\|Q^{\eta,\zeta}_{0\mid h}),
\end{align*}
where the last inequality uses nonnegativity and contraction of KL under the OU kernel.
Tonelli's theorem and \cref{eq:integrated-predictive-kl} finish the proof.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:smooth-latent-score-class}
Fix $n$ and a latent grid, and choose tolerances
\begin{equation*}
 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor},n},\ \varepsilon_{\ell,n},\
 \varepsilon_{a,n},\ \varepsilon_{b,n},\
 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{rat},n}\in(0,1].
\end{equation*}
Also fix a deterministic final output radius $0<R_{\mathrm{out},n}\le R_{\mathrm{bal},n}$.
The radius $R_{\mathrm{bal},n}$ remains the uniform bound used for latent quadrature and internal basis estimates; $R_{\mathrm{out},n}$ controls the score-network output and the training loss.
In the input--output prompt model, also fix $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{qry},n}\in(0,1]$; in the sample-only prompt model, set $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{qry},n}:=0$ and omit the query factor below.
Index the context-example tokens by $I_{\mathrm{ctx}}:=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and the latent tokens by $I_{\mathrm{lat}}:=\{i_1^{\mathrm{lat}},\ldots,i_{J_{\theta,n}}^{\mathrm{lat}}\}$.
Let $i_{\mathrm{sum}}$, $i_{\mathrm{qry}}$, $i_{\mathrm{out}}$, and $i_{\varnothing}$ denote the summary, query--state--time, output, and null tokens, respectively.
Thus the sequence has $n+J_{\theta,n}+4$ positions.
Choose the explicit token width in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-explicit-token-width}, namely
\begin{equation*}
  d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  =(d_z+5)J_{\theta,n}+d_{\mathrm{stat}}+2d_z+11
  \le C_{d_z}\{J_{\theta,n}+d_{\mathrm{stat}}+1\}.
\end{equation*}
\emph{Trainable parameter space.}
Let ${\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}}$ be the product of the compact parameter cubes for the likelihood approximators with bounded outputs in \cref{lem:fixed-dimensional-holder-relu-approximation}, the time-local OU-basis approximators in \cref{lem:smooth-latent-time-local-basis}, and the prior logits with radius \cref{eq:smooth-latent-prior-logit-radius}.
For attention, include every entry of each of the sixteen matrices
\begin{equation*}
 W_{Q,k},W_{K,k},W_{V,k},W_{O,k}\in
 [-B_{\mathrm{att},n},B_{\mathrm{att},n}]^{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}\times d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}},
 \quad 1\le k\le4,
\end{equation*}
\begin{equation*}
 B_{\mathrm{att},n}=1\vee\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}.
\end{equation*}
Thus the attention part has exactly $16(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2$ trainable coordinates.
The sparse matrices in \cref{tab:smooth-latent-four-block-construction} specify a reference parameter within this entire score class.
The table describes the computation at that parameter; arbitrary trainable projections need not preserve its internal posterior interpretation or role coordinates.
The hard masks and coordinate-update maps are fixed for the entire class.
The query log-likelihood factor is omitted in the sample-only prompt model.
The fixed approximator architecture, latent identifiers, role codes, hard masks, coordinate-update maps, ratio readout, and radial output projection are not coordinates of ${\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}}$.
Let $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}:=\dim({\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}})$, let $B_{\mathrm{lat},n}\ge1$ dominate its coordinate radii, and let
\begin{equation*}
  \mathrm{Proj}_n^{\mathrm{par}}:
  [-B_{\mathrm{lat},n},B_{\mathrm{lat},n}]^{\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}
  \longrightarrow{\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}}
\end{equation*}
be coordinatewise projection onto the corresponding factor ranges.
This map is continuous, nonexpansive in $\ell_{\infty}$, and onto.

\emph{Token representation.}
For a parameter vector $\mathbf{v}$ in the displayed cube, put $\widehat{\mathbf{v}}:=\mathrm{Proj}_n^{\mathrm{par}}(\mathbf{v})$.
We identify ${\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}}$ with its natural subset of the enclosing cube; on this subset, $\mathrm{Proj}_n^{\mathrm{par}}$ is the identity.
Let $\mathbf{E}_n^{(0)}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}};z,t,h)$ be the encoded array of the $n$ context-example tokens, one summary token, one query--state--time token, $J_{\theta,n}$ latent tokens, one output token, and one null token.
In particular, every trainable component encoder is evaluated at the projected parameter vector $\widehat{\mathbf{v}}$.
The network-component specification in Appendix~\ref{app:subsubsec:transformer-construction} gives the initial value and shape of every token type, with ordered coordinate decomposition \cref{eq:smooth-latent-token-block-decomposition}.
The context-example network uses the fixed affine rescaling $c\mapsto2c-\mathbf{1}_{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}$.
In the input--output prompt model, the query log-likelihood network uses $x_{\star}\mapsto2x_{\star}-\mathbf{1}_{d_x}$.
The OU-basis network uses the globally bounded feature $\mathsf{u}_n(z,t,h)$ from \cref{eq:transformer-input-preprocessing}.
Its fixed time-local encoder combines overlapping time patches with a continuous ReLU partition of unity, as specified in Lemma~\ref{lem:smooth-latent-time-local-basis}; the four attention blocks are unchanged.
All occurrences of the noisy state in this encoded array use $\bar{z}={\mathrm{clip}}_{R_{\mathrm{state},n}}(z)$; there is no unprocessed state input.
Consequently, for every parameter in the class,
\begin{equation}
 \widetilde{s}_{\mathbf{v}}(z,t,h)=\widetilde{s}_{\mathbf{v}}(\bar{z},t,h).
 \label{eq:global-state-clipping-reduction}
\end{equation}
This identity reduces the full-state supremum in $d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty}$ to the compact state ball.
We suppress the common arguments $(\widehat{\mathbf{v}};z,t,h)$ from the token arrays in the recursion below.

\emph{Attention blocks.}
For $1\le k\le4$ and every token row $i$, define
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k)}
  :=
  \mathsf{R}_{k,n}\!\left[
  \mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k-1)}
  {}+
  \operatorname{Att}_{\mathbf{A}_k(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})}
  \!\left(
  \mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k-1)};
  (\mathbf{E}_{n,j}^{(k-1)})_{j\in\mathsf{M}_{k,n}(i)}
  \right)
  \right].
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-score-class-recursion}
\end{equation}
Here $\mathbf{A}_k(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})$ denotes the four dense bounded attention projection matrices, and $\mathsf{R}_{k,n}$ is the fixed affine--ReLU coordinate-update map after block $k$.
The non-null masks are
\begin{equation*}
  \mathsf{M}_{1,n}(i_{\mathrm{sum}})=I_{\mathrm{ctx}},
  \qquad
  \mathsf{M}_{2,n}(i_j^{\mathrm{lat}})=\{i_{\mathrm{sum}}\},
  \qquad
  \mathsf{M}_{3,n}(i_j^{\mathrm{lat}})=\{i_{\mathrm{qry}}\},
  \qquad
  \mathsf{M}_{4,n}(i_{\mathrm{out}})=I_{\mathrm{lat}},
\end{equation*}
for $i_j^{\mathrm{lat}}\in I_{\mathrm{lat}}$, and every undeclared row attends only to $i_{\varnothing}$.
The projection matrices of the constructed network, residual destinations, and states after $\mathsf{R}_{k,n}$ are listed in \cref{tab:smooth-latent-four-block-construction}; bounded coordinatewise projection precedes the exact selector in \cref{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-binary-gate}.

\emph{Score readout and class.}
Let $\mathsf{Read}_n$ first apply exact coordinatewise projection onto
\begin{equation*}
 [\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}]\times
 [-R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}-1,R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}+1]^{d_z},
\end{equation*}
and then apply the fixed affine normalization and ReLU ratio approximator constructed below.
This preliminary projection is part of the readout for every parameter; it is inactive on the reference network's averaged features.
Let $\mathsf{P}_{\mathrm{out}}$ extract the output-token coordinates.
Define
\begin{equation}
  \widetilde{s}_{\mathbf{v}}(z,t,h)
  :=
  {\mathrm{clip}}_{R_{\mathrm{out},n}}
  \!\left[
  \mathsf{Read}_n\!\left(
  \mathsf{P}_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf{E}_n^{(4)}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}};z,t,h)
  \right)
  \right],
  \qquad
  s_{\mathbf{v}}(z,t,h):=\frac{\widetilde{s}_{\mathbf{v}}(z,t,h)}{\sigma_t},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-score-class-output}
\end{equation}
and set
\begin{equation}
  {\mathcal{S}}_n^{\mathrm{lat}}
  :=
  \left\{
  s_{\mathbf{v}}:
  \mathbf{v}\in
  [-B_{\mathrm{lat},n},B_{\mathrm{lat},n}]^{\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}
  \right\}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-score-class-formal}
\end{equation}
Writing $\mathbf{A}_k(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})=(W_{Q,k},W_{K,k},W_{V,k},W_{O,k})$, the attention map in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-score-class-recursion} is $\operatorname{Att}_{W_{Q,k},W_{K,k},W_{V,k},W_{O,k}}$ from \cref{lem:attention-parameter-sensitivity} restricted to the displayed deterministic mask.
Here ``Transformer'' means the explicitly defined prompt-conditioned architecture: external ReLU input and readout modules surround four hard-masked residual softmax-attention blocks with fixed affine--ReLU coordinate updates and no layer normalization.
Exact containment in a fixed-width conventional Transformer with mandatory feed-forward sublayers or layer normalization is not claimed.
In particular, the definition does not cover a standard pre-normalized or post-normalized Transformer with fully learned coordinate updates.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-four-block-construction}
For the encoded array above, there exist four deterministic nonempty masks, projection matrices for the constructed network
\begin{equation*}
  W_{Q,k},W_{K,k},W_{V,k},W_{O,k}
  \in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}\times d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}},
  \qquad 1\le k\le4,
\end{equation*}
and shared rowwise affine--ReLU maps
\begin{equation*}
  \mathsf{R}_{k,n}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}
\end{equation*}
such that each residual block maps ${\mathbb{R}}^{(n+J+4)\times d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}$ to itself and implements the computation in \cref{tab:smooth-latent-four-block-construction}.
Every row not listed in that table attends only to the null token.
After block 4, the output block equals
\begin{equation}
  \left(
  \sum_{j=1}^J\widehat{\omega}_{j,n}\widehat{\mathsf{a}}_j,
  \sum_{j=1}^J\widehat{\omega}_{j,n}\widehat{\mathsf{b}}_j
  \right)\in\mathsf{V}_{\mathrm{out}},
  \qquad
  \widehat{\omega}_{j,n}
  =\operatorname{softmax}_j((\widehat{\ell}_{k,n}^{\mathrm{post}})_{k=1}^{J}),
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-four-block-output}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
  \widehat{\ell}_{j,n}^{\mathrm{post}}
  =\ell_{j,n}^{\mathrm{prior}}
  +n\overline{\widehat{\ell}}_j
  +\widehat{\ell}_j^{\mathrm{qry}},
  \qquad
  \overline{\widehat{\ell}}_j
  :=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n\widehat{\ell}_j(c_i).
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-posterior-logit-update}
\end{equation}
The four residual updates preserve all role codes and latent identifiers.
Every projection and coordinate-update coefficient of the constructed network has magnitude at most
\begin{equation*}
  1\vee n\vee B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  \vee\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}},
\end{equation*}
where $B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ is defined in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-selector-radius}.
Hence the construction is an explicit member of the score class in \cref{def:smooth-latent-score-class}.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
All reference matrices are compositions of coordinate projections and injections associated with \cref{eq:smooth-latent-token-block-decomposition}, except for the scalar factors $n$ and $\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}$ described below.
For block 1, set the active query and key projections to zero, project the context-example values from $\mathsf{V}_{\ell}$, and inject the attention output into $\mathsf{V}_{\mathrm{pool}}$.
The summary mask contains exactly the $n$ context-example rows, so softmax attention returns their mean.
For blocks 2 and 3, the active key set of each latent row is a singleton, so the attention weight is exactly one independently of its logit.
Coordinate projection and injection therefore copy the summary pool and the full query--basis array into the indicated blocks of every latent row.

The fixed map $\mathsf{R}_{3,n}$ projects every selector input onto $[-B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}},B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}]$ and uses the one-hot identifier $e_j$.
For $e\in\{0,1\}$ and $|x|\le B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, the exact affine--ReLU identity
\begin{equation}
  ex=\operatorname{ReLU}(x)
  -\operatorname{ReLU}(x-B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}e)
  -\operatorname{ReLU}(-x)
  +\operatorname{ReLU}(-x-B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}e)
  \label{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-binary-gate}
\end{equation}
selects one coordinate exactly.
Parallel copies select the $j$th pooled likelihood, query likelihood, scalar basis value, and vector basis value.
An affine output layer then forms \cref{eq:smooth-latent-posterior-logit-update} and writes the resulting $(d_z+2)$-vector into $\mathsf{V}_{\mathrm{lat}}$.
The six one-hot role codes and the same binary-gate identity make these tokenwise coordinate updates shared across rows while leaving all other coordinate blocks unchanged.

For block 4, map the output-token role coordinate to $\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}e_1$ and map latent token $j$ to $\widehat{\ell}_{j,n}^{\mathrm{post}}e_1$ in key space.
The scaled dot product is therefore exactly $\widehat{\ell}_{j,n}^{\mathrm{post}}$.
Map the remaining latent coordinates to $(\widehat{\mathsf{a}}_j,\widehat{\mathsf{b}}_j)$ and inject the weighted attention output into $\mathsf{V}_{\mathrm{out}}$.
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-four-block-output} and every row of \cref{tab:smooth-latent-four-block-construction}.
The null-token value is zero for all four value projections of the constructed network, so unlisted rows receive zero attention updates.
The direct-sum dimensions add to \cref{eq:smooth-latent-explicit-token-width}, and the displayed coefficient bound follows from the coordinate maps, selector threshold, posterior-logit multiplier, and scaling used in the dot product.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:attention-parameter-sensitivity}
Fix $B\ge1$.
Suppose every entry of the tokens and matrices in this attention map and in a primed copy is bounded in absolute value by $B$.
Let $\eta_{\mathrm{att}}$ be the maximum of the entrywise matrix differences and the $\ell_{\infty}$ distances between corresponding tokens.
Then
\begin{equation*}
  \left\|
  \operatorname{Att}_{W_Q,W_K,W_V,W_O}(v;x_{1:m})
  -\operatorname{Att}_{W'_Q,W'_K,W'_V,W'_O}(v';x'_{1:m})
  \right\|_{\infty}
  \le16d^5B^6\eta_{\mathrm{att}}.
\end{equation*}
The bound is independent of the number of keys $m$ and remains valid after restricting both maps by the same deterministic nonempty attention mask.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
The matrix inequality $\|Ax\|_{\infty}\le d\|A\|_{\max}\|x\|_{\infty}$ gives
\begin{equation*}
  \|W_Qv-W'_Qv'\|_{\infty}
  +\max_j\|W_Kx_j-W'_Kx_j'\|_{\infty}
  \le4dB\eta_{\mathrm{att}}.
\end{equation*}
A two-term telescoping argument for the scaled dot products therefore gives
\begin{equation*}
  \max_j\left|
  \frac{(W_Qv)^{\top} W_Kx_j-(W'_Qv')^{\top} W'_Kx_j'}{\sqrt{d}}
  \right|
  \le4d^{5/2}B^3\eta_{\mathrm{att}}.
\end{equation*}
The softmax map satisfies the following Lipschitzness~\citep[Corollary A.7]{edelman22a}:
\begin{equation*}
  \|\operatorname{softmax}(a)-\operatorname{softmax}(a')\|_1
  \le2\|a-a'\|_{\infty}.
\end{equation*}
The weighted value average consequently changes by at most
\begin{equation*}
  \left\|
  \sum_j\pi_jW_Vx_j-
  \sum_j\pi_j'W'_Vx_j'
  \right\|_{\infty}
  \le\{8d^{7/2}B^5+2dB\}\eta_{\mathrm{att}}.
\end{equation*}
One final telescoping step through $W_O$ yields
\begin{equation*}
  \|W_O-W'_O\|_{\max}\left\|\sum_j\pi_jW_Vx_j\right\|_1
  +dB\left\|\sum_j\pi_jW_Vx_j-\sum_j\pi_j'W'_Vx_j'\right\|_{\infty}
  \le16d^5B^6\eta_{\mathrm{att}}.
\end{equation*}
Applying the same argument to the restricted list of keys selected by the common mask proves the masked version.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:fixed-dimensional-holder-relu-approximation}
Fix $d,d_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\beta_0\in(0,1]$.
There is an exponent $0<c<\infty$, depending only on $(d,d_{\mathrm{out}},\beta_0)$, with the following property; all implicit constants below have the same allowed dependencies.
Let $M_f,H\ge0$, let $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, and let $f:[-1,1]^d\to[-M_f,M_f]^{d_{\mathrm{out}}}$ satisfy
\begin{equation*}
  \|f(x)-f(x')\|_{\infty}
  \le H\|x-x'\|_{\infty}^{\beta_0}.
\end{equation*}
There are integers $D_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{NN}},W_{\varepsilon},S_{\varepsilon}\ge1$, a bound $B_{\varepsilon}\ge1$, and a fixed-architecture class
\begin{equation*}
  \mathscr{N}_{\varepsilon}
  \subseteq
  \mathrm{Proj}_{[-M_f,M_f]^{d_{\mathrm{out}}}}\circ
  {\mathcal{NN}}(D_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{NN}},W_{\varepsilon},S_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}),
\end{equation*}
chosen independently of $f$, that contains a member $f_{\varepsilon}$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
  \sup_{x\in[-1,1]^d}
  \|f_{\varepsilon}(x)-f(x)\|_{\infty}\le\varepsilon.
\end{equation*}
The network sizes satisfy
\begin{equation}
  D_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{NN}}\vee W_{\varepsilon}\vee S_{\varepsilon}
  \lesssim
  \left(1+\frac{H\vee1}{\varepsilon}\right)^{d/\beta_0}
  \log^2\!\left(2+\frac{1+M_f+H}{\varepsilon}\right),
  \label{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-network-size}
\end{equation}
Every scalar weight and bias has magnitude at most $B_{\varepsilon}$.
The tensor-hat and multiplication subnetworks are fixed, and only the output-layer interpolation coefficients vary over a compact cube.
After adding functionally inactive coordinates, write the class as
\begin{equation*}
  \mathscr{N}_{\varepsilon}
  =\{f_{\vartheta}:\vartheta\in[-B_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}]^{S_{\varepsilon}}\}.
\end{equation*}
The evaluation map $(x,\vartheta)\mapsto f_{\vartheta}(x)$ is jointly Borel and continuous in $\vartheta$.
There are constants $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{par}},\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{in}}\in[0,\infty)$ such that
\begin{align*}
  \sup_x\|f_{\vartheta}(x)-f_{\vartheta'}(x)\|_{\infty}
  &\le \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{par}}\|\vartheta-\vartheta'\|_{\infty},
  \\
  \sup_{\vartheta\in[-B_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}]^{S_{\varepsilon}}}
  \|f_{\vartheta}(x)-f_{\vartheta}(x')\|_{\infty}
  &\le \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{in}}\|x-x'\|_{\infty},
\end{align*}
and
\begin{equation}
  B_{\varepsilon}+\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{par}}+\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{in}}
  \lesssim (1+M_f+H+\varepsilon^{-1})^c.
  \label{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Affinely rescale the input cube to $[0,1]^d$ and take a regular tensor grid with mesh
\begin{equation*}
  \eta_{\mathrm{grid}}:=\min\left\{1,
  c_d\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{H\vee1}\right)^{1/\beta_0}\right\},
\end{equation*}
where $c_d>0$ is chosen below.
If $u_1,\ldots,u_{J_{\mathrm{hat}}}$ are the grid points and $\psi_1,\ldots,\psi_{J_{\mathrm{hat}}}$ the tensor-hat partition of unity, then $J_{\mathrm{hat}}\le C_d\{1+(H\vee1)/\varepsilon\}^{d/\beta_0}$.
The interpolant
\begin{equation*}
  I_{\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}}f(x):=\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathrm{hat}}} f(u_j)\psi_j(x)
\end{equation*}
satisfies $\|I_{\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}}f-f\|_{\infty}\le H(C_d\eta_{\mathrm{grid}})^{\beta_0}\le\varepsilon/2$ after decreasing $c_d$.

Every univariate hat is an exact ReLU function.
Use the ReLU multiplication construction in \citet[Proposition~3 and its proof]{yarotsky2017} on a fixed bounded interval, recursively approximate each product of $d$ univariate hats, and project the result onto $[0,1]$.
With
\begin{equation*}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}:=\frac{\varepsilon}{2J_{\mathrm{hat}}(M_f\vee1)},
\end{equation*}
each approximate tensor hat $\widehat{\psi}_j$ can be chosen so that $\|\widehat{\psi}_j-\psi_j\|_{\infty}\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}$.
Consequently,
\begin{equation*}
  \left\|
  \sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathrm{hat}}}f(u_j)\widehat{\psi}_j-I_{\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}}f
  \right\|_{\infty}
  \le J_{\mathrm{hat}}M_f\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}\le\varepsilon/2.
\end{equation*}
The multiplication paths have fixed width and depth ${\mathcal{O}}_d\!\left(\log(2+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}^{-1})\right)$.
Parallelizing the $J_{\mathrm{hat}}$ subnetworks gives a fixed wiring pattern whose depth, width, and total number of scalar weights and biases obey \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-network-size}; the extra logarithm allows all coordinates to share one network graph.
Fix every hat and multiplication weight at the value used in these $J_{\mathrm{hat}}$ approximate basis functions.
For a coefficient array $\mathbf{a}=(a_{kj})_{1\le k\le d_{\mathrm{out}},1\le j\le J_{\mathrm{hat}}}$, define
\begin{equation*}
  \widetilde{f}_{\mathbf{a}}(x)
  :=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{J_{\mathrm{hat}}} a_{kj}\widehat{\psi}_j(x)\right)_{k=1}^{d_{\mathrm{out}}},
  \qquad
  f_{\mathbf{a}}:=\mathrm{Proj}_{[-M_f,M_f]^{d_{\mathrm{out}}}}\circ\widetilde{f}_{\mathbf{a}}.
\end{equation*}
Choose $B_{\varepsilon}$ to bound the fixed network weights, the hat slopes, and the coefficient range $[-B_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}]^{d_{\mathrm{out}}J_{\mathrm{hat}}}$, with $B_{\varepsilon}\ge M_f$.
Only $\mathbf{a}$ is variable; all internal network parameters are fixed and therefore are not trainable.
Pad $\mathbf{a}$ by inactive coordinates to obtain a parameter vector $\vartheta\in[-B_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}]^{S_{\varepsilon}}$.
The resulting class and its fixed wiring depend only on $(d,d_{\mathrm{out}},\beta_0,M_f,H,\varepsilon)$ and not on $f$.
Taking $a_{kj}=f_k(u_j)$ gives the approximant established above.

Because $0\le\widehat{\psi}_j\le1$ and Euclidean projection onto the box is nonexpansive,
\begin{equation*}
  \sup_x\|f_{\mathbf{a}}(x)-f_{\mathbf{a}'}(x)\|_{\infty}
  \le J_{\mathrm{hat}}\|\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}'\|_{\infty}.
\end{equation*}
Thus one may take $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{par}}=J_{\mathrm{hat}}$.
Each exact univariate hat is $C_d\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}^{-1}$-Lipschitz.
The multiplication paths have fixed width, uniformly bounded weights, and depth ${\mathcal{O}}_d(\log(2+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}^{-1}))$.
Write $\operatorname{Lip}_{\infty}(g)$ for the Lipschitz constant of a map $g$ with respect to the $\ell_{\infty}$ norm.
For a constant $\kappa_d>0$ depending only on $d$, the layerwise operator-norm bound gives
\begin{equation*}
  \max_{1\le j\le J_{\mathrm{hat}}}\operatorname{Lip}_{\infty}(\widehat{\psi}_j)
  \le C_d\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}^{-1}(2+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}^{-1})^{\kappa_d}
  =:\mathsf{Lip}_{\psi,\varepsilon}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, uniformly over the coefficient cube,
\begin{equation*}
  \|f_{\mathbf{a}}(x)-f_{\mathbf{a}}(x')\|_{\infty}
  \le J_{\mathrm{hat}}B_{\varepsilon}\mathsf{Lip}_{\psi,\varepsilon}\|x-x'\|_{\infty}.
\end{equation*}
Hence one may take $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{in}}=J_{\mathrm{hat}}B_{\varepsilon}\mathsf{Lip}_{\psi,\varepsilon}$.
The quantities $J_{\mathrm{hat}}$, $\eta_{\mathrm{grid}}^{-1}$, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mult}}^{-1}$, and $B_{\varepsilon}$ are polynomial in $(1+M_f+H+\varepsilon^{-1})$ because $d$ is fixed.
This proves \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability}.
All remaining measurability and continuity claims follow from finite continuous composition.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-time-local-basis}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, use the constants from \cref{lem:positive-smooth-latent-derived-regularity}, and fix $0<t_0<T$, the state radius, and $\varepsilon_{a,n},\varepsilon_{b,n}\in(0,1]$.
Define
\begin{equation*}
 K:=\max\{1,\lfloor\log_2(T/t_0)\rfloor\},\qquad
 t_j^{\mathrm{knot}}:=2^jt_0\ (0\le j<K),\qquad t_K^{\mathrm{knot}}:=T.
\end{equation*}
For $0\le j\le K$, set
\begin{equation*}
 a_j:=t_{\max\{0,j-1\}}^{\mathrm{knot}},\qquad
 b_j:=t_{\min\{K,j+1\}}^{\mathrm{knot}},\qquad
 h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}:=b_j-a_j.
\end{equation*}
Let $R_{\mathrm{geom},n}:=1+R_{\mathrm{state},n}+\sqrt{d_z}$, and fix a sufficiently large constant $C_{\mathrm{loc}}$ depending only on the fixed dimensions, $\beta'$, and the common density bounds.
Apply the extension construction of \cref{lem:smooth-latent-normalized-basis-regularity} on each interval, using the query and state normalization in \cref{eq:transformer-input-preprocessing} and affine normalization of the local time interval to $[-1,1]$.
The resulting denominator and numerator maps have respective $\beta'$-H\"older moduli bounded by
\begin{align}
 H_{a,j,n}
 &:=C_{\mathrm{loc}}\big[
 1+H_{q,x}
 +R_{\mathrm{state},n}R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{a_j}^{-2}
 +h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{a_j}^{-4}\big],
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-local-denominator-modulus}\\
 H_{b,j,n}
 &:=C_{\mathrm{loc}}\big[
 1+R_{\mathrm{bal},n}H_{q,x}
 +R_{\mathrm{state},n}
   \{\sigma_{a_j}^{-1}+R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^2\sigma_{a_j}^{-3}\}
 \nonumber\\
 &\hspace{18mm}
 +h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}\{\sigma_{a_j}^{-1}
 +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}\sigma_{a_j}^{-3}
 +R_{\mathrm{geom},n}^3\sigma_{a_j}^{-5}\}\big].
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-local-numerator-modulus}
\end{align}
Write
\begin{equation*}
 H_{\max,n}:=\max_{0\le j\le K}(H_{a,j,n}\vee H_{b,j,n}),
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
 L_{\mathrm{bas},n}
 :=\log\!\left(
 2+K+\frac{1+R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}+H_{\max,n}}
 {\varepsilon_{a,n}\wedge\varepsilon_{b,n}}
 \right).
\end{equation*}
For each latent-grid point, there is a shared fixed-architecture ReLU class containing approximations with uniform errors at most $\varepsilon_{a,n}$ for $\mathsf{a}$ and $\varepsilon_{b,n}$ in Euclidean norm for $\mathsf{b}$ on $\mathcal{G}_n$.
Across all $J_{\theta,n}$ points, the number of trainable parameters, number of stored nonzero coefficients, and evaluation cost are bounded by
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{basis},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
 :=C_{\mathrm{loc}} J_{\theta,n}L_{\mathrm{bas},n}^2
 \sum_{j=0}^K\left[
 \left(1+\frac{H_{a,j,n}}{\varepsilon_{a,n}}\right)^{(d_{\mathrm{stat}}+1)/\beta'}
 +d_z\left(1+\frac{H_{b,j,n}}{\varepsilon_{b,n}}\right)^{(d_{\mathrm{stat}}+1)/\beta'}
 \right].
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-time-local-basis-size}
\end{equation}
Every member is projected onto $[\underline{q}_{\mathrm{out}},\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}]$ in its denominator coordinate and onto $[-R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}-1,R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}+1]$ in each numerator coordinate.
Only output-layer interpolation coefficients are trainable; they range over a compact cube, with inactive padding coordinates allowed.
The parameter radius and the input and parameter Lipschitz constants are polynomial in
\begin{equation*}
 1+K+T+t_0^{-1}+R_{\mathrm{state},n}
 +R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}+H_{\max,n}
 +(\varepsilon_{a,n}\wedge\varepsilon_{b,n})^{-1}.
\end{equation*}
The class depends only on the displayed common quantities, not on the task map or task prior.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The covariance calculation in \cref{app:subsubsec:diffusion-feature-regularity}, in particular \cref{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-covariance-identity}, applies on every interval $[a_j,b_j]$ with $\sigma_{t_0}$ replaced by $\sigma_{a_j}$.
Affine time normalization multiplies the time derivative by $h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}/2$.
The denominator has query modulus $H_{q,x}$, whereas the numerator has query modulus at most $R_{\mathrm{bal},n}H_{q,x}$.
Combining these observations with the separate state and time derivatives proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-local-denominator-modulus,eq:smooth-latent-local-numerator-modulus}.

Let $\chi_0,\ldots,\chi_K$ be the continuous piecewise-linear nodal hats on the knots $t_0^{\mathrm{knot}},\ldots,t_K^{\mathrm{knot}}$, restricted to $[t_0,T]$.
They are nonnegative, sum to one, and $\chi_j$ is supported on $[a_j,b_j]$.
Each is an exact affine--ReLU function of the globally normalized time coordinate.
The final interval includes any shorter remaining interval: when $K\ge2$, consecutive knot gaps are at least $t_0$ and $h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}\le7a_j$.
When $K=1$, both supports are the whole interval and normalization cancels $T-t_0$.
Thus the hats and the local coordinates after coordinatewise projection
\begin{equation*}
 u_{t,j}:=
 2\min\{1,\max\{0,(t-a_j)/h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}\}\}-1
\end{equation*}
have coefficients and input Lipschitz constants polynomial in $1+T+t_0^{-1}$ when expressed in the globally normalized input.

Consider one scalar function $f$, with range bound $B:=1+\overline{q}_{\mathrm{out}}+R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ and desired error $\varepsilon$.
Use $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{a,n}$ for the denominator and $\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{b,n}/\sqrt{d_z}$ for each numerator coordinate.
On the $j$th normalized cube, construct the tensor-hat interpolant $I_jf$ from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-dimensional-holder-relu-approximation}, with error at most $\varepsilon/2$.
Let $H_{f,j,n}$ be $H_{a,j,n}$ for the denominator and $H_{b,j,n}$ for a numerator coordinate.
Its number of grid points is $\lesssim(1+H_{f,j,n}/\varepsilon)^{(d_{\mathrm{stat}}+1)/\beta'}$.
Write its tensor hats as $\psi_{j,r}$ and its nodal coefficients as $c_{j,r}$.
Then
\begin{equation*}
 I f:=\sum_{j,r}c_{j,r}\chi_j(t)\psi_{j,r}(u_x,u_z,u_{t,j})
\end{equation*}
has error at most $\varepsilon/2$: every nonzero $\chi_j$ uses a local interpolant valid at that time, and their weights sum to one.

Let $J_f^{\mathrm{loc}}$ be the total number of these local grid points.
Each product $\chi_j\psi_{j,r}$ has $d_{\mathrm{stat}}+2$ bounded univariate factors.
Use the fixed multiplication paths in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:fixed-dimensional-holder-relu-approximation} and project their outputs onto $[0,1]$.
Approximate each product to accuracy $\varepsilon/\{2J_f^{\mathrm{loc}}(B\vee1)\}$.
The accumulated error after weighting by the interpolant coefficients is at most $\varepsilon/2$.
Only $c_{j,r}$ vary, and the final coordinatewise projections cannot increase error.
The number of coefficients and operations is $\lesssim J_f^{\mathrm{loc}}\log^2(2+J_f^{\mathrm{loc}} B/\varepsilon)$.
Summing the coordinates and latent-grid points gives \cref{eq:smooth-latent-time-local-basis-size} after absorbing fixed dimension factors.

For arbitrary trainable coefficients, every approximate hat remains in $[0,1]$, so parameter sensitivity is at most $J_f^{\mathrm{loc}}$ before the final nonexpansive projection.
The univariate hat slopes and time-coordinate maps have polynomial bounds.
The multiplication paths have fixed width, bounded weights, and logarithmic depth; their input Lipschitz constants are therefore polynomial in the displayed quantities, by the same layerwise bound used in \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability}.
Summing $J_f^{\mathrm{loc}}$ paths with bounded output-layer coefficients preserves polynomial input sensitivity.
Running the component networks in parallel and adding inactive coordinates yields the stated common compact parameter cube.
All computations are continuous, so evaluation is jointly Borel and continuous in the parameters.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-output-clipping}
For a deterministic radius $R_{\mathrm{out},n}>0$, set
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{out,clip},n}^2
 :=A_{t_0,T}{\mathbb{E}}(\|\xi\|_2-R_{\mathrm{out},n})_{+}^2,
 \qquad \xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d_z}).
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-output-clipping-error}
\end{equation}
Under the episodic OU model,
\begin{equation}
 {\mathbb{E}}_{H_n}\int_{t_0}^T
 \frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_t\mid H_n}
 \|\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_t,t\mid H_n)
 -{\mathrm{clip}}_{R_{\mathrm{out},n}}\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_t,t\mid H_n)\|_2^2}
 {\sigma_t^2}\,{\mathrm{d}} t
 \le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{out,clip},n}^2.
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-output-clipping-bias}
\end{equation}
If $R_{\mathrm{out},n}\ge\sqrt{d_z}$, then
\begin{equation}
 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{out,clip},n}^2
 \le2A_{t_0,T}
 \exp\!\left\{-\tfrac{1}{2}(R_{\mathrm{out},n}-\sqrt{d_z})^2\right\}.
 \label{eq:smooth-latent-output-clipping-tail}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
The denoising identity gives $\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_t,t\mid H_n)=-{\mathbb{E}}[\xi\mid Z_t,H_n]$.
The squared distance to a closed convex ball is convex.
Conditional Jensen therefore bounds its expectation at this conditional mean by ${\mathbb{E}}(\|\xi\|_2-R_{\mathrm{out},n})_{+}^2$.
Integration against $\sigma_t^{-2}{\mathrm{d}} t$ proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-output-clipping-bias}.
More generally, Gaussian norm concentration gives, for every $R\ge\sqrt{d_z}$ and $r=R-\sqrt{d_z}$,
\begin{equation}
 {\mathbb{E}}(\|\xi\|_2-R)_{+}^2
 \le\int_0^\infty 2u e^{-(r+u)^2/2}\,{\mathrm{d}} u
 \le2e^{-r^2/2}.
 \label{eq:gaussian-radial-tail-moment}
\end{equation}
Taking $R=R_{\mathrm{out},n}$ proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-output-clipping-tail}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:integrated-transformer-approximation}
Use the regular grid and cell-midpoint map from \cref{app:subsec:latent-quadrature}, set $\eta=\eta_{\theta,n}$ and $\zeta=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor},n}>0$ in \cref{prop:episode-kl-score-approximation}, and use the class in \cref{def:smooth-latent-score-class}.
Let $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}$ be the component-error expression in \cref{app:subsec:shared-neural-estimates}.
Then
\begin{align}
 {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{app}}({\mathcal{S}}_n^{\mathrm{lat}})
 \le {}&4D_n(\eta_{\theta,n},\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor},n})
       +4A_{t_0,T}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}^2
       +2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{out,clip},n}^2 \nonumber\\
       &+2A_{t_0,T}R_{\mathrm{out},n}^2\delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
       +2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2.
 \label{eq:integrated-transformer-approximation}
\end{align}
The parameters of the approximating network may depend on the task family and prior.
Its architecture, coordinate bounds, and approximation guarantee depend only on the common bounds, grid, and tolerances.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
By \cref{eq:regular-grid-floored-score-identity}, the construction in \cref{app:subsubsec:shared-neural-approximation}, in particular \cref{eq:smooth-latent-network-error}, gives an output $f$ before the final radial projection satisfying $\|f-\sigma_t s^{\eta_{\theta,n},\zeta}\|_2\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}$ on $\mathcal{G}_n$. 
No uniform comparison of $s^{\eta_{\theta,n},\zeta}$ with $s^\star$ is used here.
Recall that $\widetilde{s}^\star=\sigma_t s^\star$, and let $\mathsf{P}$ be radial projection onto the ball of radius $R_{\mathrm{out},n}$.
On $\mathcal{G}_n$, nonexpansiveness and two applications of the squared triangle inequality give
\begin{equation*}
 \|\mathsf{P} f-\widetilde{s}^\star\|_2^2
 \le4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}^2
   +4\sigma_t^2\|s^{\eta_{\theta,n},\zeta}-s^\star\|_2^2
   +2\|\mathsf{P}\widetilde{s}^\star-\widetilde{s}^\star\|_2^2.
\end{equation*}
Integrate against $\sigma_t^{-2}P_{t\mid H_n}\,{\mathrm{d}} t$ and the original prompt law.
The first term integrates to at most $4A_{t_0,T}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}^2$, the second is bounded by \cref{eq:integrated-latent-score-bound}, and the third by \cref{lem:smooth-latent-output-clipping}.
On $\mathcal{G}_n^c$, use $\|\mathsf{P} f-\widetilde{s}^\star\|^2\le2R_{\mathrm{out},n}^2+2\|\widetilde{s}^\star\|^2$ and the two bounds in \cref{lem:balanced-score-truncation}.
This proves \cref{eq:integrated-transformer-approximation}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:smooth-latent-complexity}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, fix any regular latent grid and component tolerances satisfying \cref{def:smooth-latent-score-class}.
Let $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ count trainable scalar coordinates, let $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{store},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ count stored nonzero scalar coefficients under this convention, and let $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{op},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ count sparse arithmetic operations in one forward evaluation.
With the indexing in \cref{def:smooth-latent-score-class}, the score class has the compact trainable-parameter representation
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathcal{S}}_n^{\mathrm{lat}}
  =\{s_{\mathbf{v}}:\mathbf{v}\in[-B_{\mathrm{lat},n},B_{\mathrm{lat},n}]^{\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}\}.
\end{equation*}
There is a deterministic constant $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}$ such that
\begin{equation}
  d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty}(s_{\mathbf{v}},s_{\mathbf{v}'})
  \le
  \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}
  \|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}'\|_{\infty}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-parameter-sensitivity}
\end{equation}
This bound holds for all parameter vectors $\mathbf{v}$ and $\mathbf{v}'$ in the displayed cube.
Consequently, for every $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}>0$,
\begin{equation}
  \logN_{\mathrm{cov}}\!\left(
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathcal{S}}_n^{\mathrm{lat}},d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty}
  \right)
  \le
  \mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  \log\!\left(
  1+\frac{2B_{\mathrm{lat},n}
  \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}}
  \right).
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-score-cover}
\end{equation}
The empirical variance-balanced DSM loss admits a Borel-measurable exact ERM over this compact parameter cube.
Explicit bounds for $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{store},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, and $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{op},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ are given in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-trainable-parameter-count,eq:smooth-latent-coefficient-storage,eq:smooth-latent-forward-cost}.
These conclusions hold uniformly over the task maps in the fixed regularity class of \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} and every Borel task prior on ${\mathcal{T}}$.
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-four-block-sensitivity}
Let $\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}'\in{\mathcal{V}}_n^{\mathrm{fit}}$ collect trainable coordinates of the component networks, prior logits, and entries of the attention projection matrices.
All token-array bounds below hold uniformly over $(h,z,t)\in\mathcal{G}_n$, and their common input arguments are suppressed.
There are deterministic quantities $B_{0,n},\mathsf{Lip}_{0,n},B_{\mathrm{par},n}\ge1$ and fixed-map constants $B_{\mathrm{rt},k,n},\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}\ge1$, $1\le k\le4$, such that
\begin{align}
  \max_i\|\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(0)}(\mathbf{v})\|_{\infty}
  &\le B_{0,n},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-initial-token-envelope}\\
  \max_i\|\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(0)}(\mathbf{v})-\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(0)}(\mathbf{v}')\|_{\infty}
  &\le \mathsf{Lip}_{0,n}\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}'\|_{\infty},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-initial-token-sensitivity}
\end{align}
and every trainable or fixed coefficient used by an attention block has magnitude at most $B_{\mathrm{par},n}$.
Define recursively, for $1\le k\le4$,
\begin{align}
  \overline{B}_{k-1,n}
  &:=\max\{1,B_{\mathrm{par},n},B_{k-1,n}\},
  \nonumber\\
  B_{k,n}
  &:=B_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}\left\{1+B_{k-1,n}+(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2\overline{B}_{k-1,n}^3\right\},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-recursion-envelope}\\
  \mathsf{Lip}_{k,n}
  &:=\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}\left[
  \mathsf{Lip}_{k-1,n}+16(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^5\overline{B}_{k-1,n}^6(1+\mathsf{Lip}_{k-1,n})
  \right].
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-recursion-sensitivity}
\end{align}
Then, for every $0\le k\le4$,
\begin{align}
  \max_i\|\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k)}(\mathbf{v})\|_{\infty}
  &\le B_{k,n},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-token-envelope}\\
  \max_i\|\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k)}(\mathbf{v})-\mathbf{E}_{n,i}^{(k)}(\mathbf{v}')\|_{\infty}
  &\le \mathsf{Lip}_{k,n}\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}'\|_{\infty}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-token-sensitivity}
\end{align}
The quantities $B_{0,n}$, $\mathsf{Lip}_{0,n}$, $B_{\mathrm{par},n}$, $B_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}$, and $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}$ are polynomial in the displayed component-network bounds, $d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, $J_{\theta,n}$, $B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, and $\log(2J_{\theta,n}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor},n})$.
If $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{out},n}$ is an input-Lipschitz constant of the fixed ratio readout followed by radial projection, then
\begin{equation}
  d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty}(s_{\mathbf{v}},s_{\mathbf{v}'})
  \le \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{out},n}\mathsf{Lip}_{4,n}\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}'\|_{\infty},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-four-block-final-sensitivity}
\end{equation}
and $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{out},n}$ is polynomial in the same inputs and $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rat},n}^{-1}$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
The likelihood-network bounds in \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability}, the time-local basis bounds in Lemma~\ref{lem:smooth-latent-time-local-basis}, the projections of component outputs, and \cref{eq:smooth-latent-prior-logit-radius} give \cref{eq:smooth-latent-initial-token-envelope,eq:smooth-latent-initial-token-sensitivity} and a polynomial choice of $B_{\mathrm{par},n}$.
After each attention update, let $\mathsf{R}_{k,n}$ denote the fixed affine--ReLU coordinate-update map, with the selector from \cref{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-binary-gate} included after the third update.
The four coordinate-update networks have a universal depth bound $D_{\mathrm{rt}}$, width at most $C_{d_z}(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2$, and coefficient magnitude at most
\begin{equation*}
  B_{\mathrm{rt},n}
  :=1\vee n\vee B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  \vee\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}.
\end{equation*}
An affine layer of width at most $w$ and coefficient bound $B$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
  1+\|Wx+b\|_{\infty}\le2w(B\vee1)(1+\|x\|_{\infty}),
  \qquad
  \|W(x-x')\|_{\infty}\le w(B\vee1)\|x-x'\|_{\infty}.
\end{equation*}
ReLU is one-Lipschitz and does not increase the bound on token magnitudes, so iteration over the $D_{\mathrm{rt}}$ layers gives constants $B_{\mathrm{rt},k,n},\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}\ge1$ satisfying
\begin{align*}
  \|\mathsf{R}_{k,n}(x)\|_{\infty}
  &\le B_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}(1+\|x\|_{\infty}),\\
  \|\mathsf{R}_{k,n}(x)-\mathsf{R}_{k,n}(x')\|_{\infty}
  &\le \mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}\|x-x'\|_{\infty}.
\end{align*}
More precisely, one may take both constants no larger than
\begin{equation*}
  \{2C_{d_z}(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2B_{\mathrm{rt},n}\}^{D_{\mathrm{rt}}}.
\end{equation*}
This proves the asserted polynomial dependence for the coordinate-update networks.
If the tokens and attention matrices entering block $k$ are bounded by $\overline{B}_{k-1,n}$, then its attention output is bounded coordinatewise by $(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2\overline{B}_{k-1,n}^3$.
Adding the residual and applying $\mathsf{R}_{k,n}$ proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-recursion-envelope,eq:smooth-latent-token-envelope} by induction.
At block $k$, the maximum perturbation among its tokens and trainable matrices is at most $(1+\mathsf{Lip}_{k-1,n})\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}'\|_{\infty}$.
The attention perturbation bound in \cref{lem:attention-parameter-sensitivity}, followed by the residual update and the $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{rt},k,n}$-Lipschitz coordinate-update map, gives \cref{eq:smooth-latent-recursion-sensitivity,eq:smooth-latent-token-sensitivity}.
The preliminary readout projection maps all inputs to the compact approximation box specified above and is nonexpansive.
Thus \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability} gives a global polynomial input-Lipschitz constant for the fixed ratio readout; radial output projection is also nonexpansive.
The state-clipping identity \cref{eq:global-state-clipping-reduction} extends the token bounds from $\mathcal{G}_n$ to the full state domain.
Absorbing the fixed conversion between $\ell_{\infty}$ and Euclidean norms into $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{out},n}$ proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-four-block-final-sensitivity}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Work under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, with the constants given in \cref{lem:positive-smooth-latent-derived-regularity}.
We first count the coefficients and operations in the attention blocks and component networks.
We then use parameter sensitivity to obtain a covering bound and a measurable ERM, while tracking the dependence on the declared grid and component tolerances.

\textbf{Model size and forward-pass cost.}
Exclude the component encoders and the fixed ratio readout, but include every nonzero fixed or trainable scalar coefficient in the four attention blocks and their coordinate-update maps.
The four-attention-block network above can be stored using at most
\begin{equation}
  16(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2+C_{d_z}d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  \le C_{d_z}(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-update-storage-bound}
\end{equation}
such coefficients and evaluated using at most
\begin{equation}
  C_{d_z}(n+J_{\theta,n}+4)(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-update-operation-bound}
\end{equation}
sparse arithmetic operations.
Structural zeros, masks, role labels, latent-cell indices, and their bit encodings are excluded under the counting convention of \cref{prop:smooth-latent-complexity}.
Every fixed coordinate-update coefficient has magnitude at most
\begin{equation}
  1\vee n\vee B_{\mathrm{gate},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}\vee\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-update-coefficient-bound}
\end{equation}



Each block has four $d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}\times d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ attention projections, so all four blocks contain at most $16(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2$ nonzero projection coefficients.
The role and block injections, coordinate copies, residual writes, scalar projections, and posterior-logit affine sum use at most $C_{d_z}d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ shared nonzero coefficients.
For the selector, one copy of \cref{eq:appendix-smooth-latent-binary-gate} is used for each of the $J_{\theta,n}$ candidate entries in each of the $d_z+2$ selected likelihood-and-basis coordinates.
It therefore uses at most $C_{d_z}J_{\theta,n}\le C_{d_z}d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ shared nonzero coefficients.
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-update-storage-bound}.

For a forward evaluation, the number of operations needed to apply the attention projections in all four blocks is $\lesssim(n+J_{\theta,n}+4)(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2$.
The masks in \cref{tab:smooth-latent-four-block-construction} contain $n+3J_{\theta,n}$ non-null query--key pairs, whose dot products and value aggregation require $\lesssim(n+J_{\theta,n})d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ further operations.
The shared sparse coordinate-update maps require at most $C_{d_z}d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ operations per token row, including the $J_{\theta,n}(d_z+2)$ selector gates on a latent row.
Residual additions and fixed coordinate injections cost $\lesssim(n+J_{\theta,n}+4)d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ operations.
Since $d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}\ge1$, these bounds imply \cref{eq:smooth-latent-update-operation-bound}.
Among the fixed coordinate-update coefficients, only the factor $n$ in the posterior logit and the projection and gate thresholds can exceed one.
The trainable attention coefficients are bounded by $1\vee\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}$ by the parameter-space definition.
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-update-coefficient-bound}.


We now add the component networks to these attention-block counts.
The trainable output-layer coefficients of the context-example log-likelihood, query log-likelihood, and OU-basis networks are bounded, up to a fixed dimension-dependent factor, by
\begin{equation*}
  \mathsf{N}_{\ell,n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  +\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{qry},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
  +\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{basis},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}.
\end{equation*}
The fixed nonzero internal coefficients of these three network families obey the same bound.
The prior contributes $J_{\theta,n}$ trainable logits.
By \cref{eq:smooth-latent-update-storage-bound}, the fixed selector and coordinate-update networks and the trainable attention matrices contribute $\lesssim(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^2$ nonzero stored coordinates.
The fixed ratio readout contributes at most $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{rat},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ nonzero stored coordinates.
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-trainable-parameter-count,eq:smooth-latent-coefficient-storage}.
Evaluating the shared context-example network $n$ times and the query networks and ratio readout once, and then applying \cref{eq:smooth-latent-update-operation-bound}, proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-forward-cost}.
In particular, only the summary row attends to all $n$ context-example tokens, the two coordinate-copy stages use singleton-key rows, and the output row attends to the $J_{\theta,n}$ latent tokens; no dense quadratic all-token attention matrix is evaluated.

\textbf{Covering numbers and measurable empirical minimization.}
To pass from the parameter count to a covering number, we bound the parameter radius and the sensitivity of the score to its parameters.
The final posterior-attention logit uses the standard $(d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}})^{-1/2}$ scaled dot product, so one fixed query or key coordinate is multiplied by $\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{emb},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}}$; this factor is included in the fixed attention and coordinate-update radius.
By construction, the radii of the parameter-space factors in \cref{def:smooth-latent-score-class} are the component-network parameter radii, the prior-logit radius in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-prior-logit-radius}, and the attention-projection radius containing the reference matrices.
Take $B_{\mathrm{lat},n}$ to be the largest of these radii.
This choice is polynomial in the displayed inputs and $\log(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor},n}^{-1})$ by \cref{eq:fixed-dimensional-holder-parameter-stability} and Lemma~\ref{lem:smooth-latent-time-local-basis}.
Because $\mathrm{Proj}_n^{\mathrm{par}}$ is nonexpansive in $\ell_{\infty}$, applying \cref{lem:smooth-latent-four-block-sensitivity} after this projection and setting $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}:=\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{out},n}\mathsf{Lip}_{4,n}$ proves the required bound.
This proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-parameter-sensitivity} and makes the claimed polynomial dependence explicit.
A grid contained in the compact trainable-parameter cube proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-score-cover}.
The resulting finite network is jointly Borel in the data and continuous in its parameters.
                                                                                                                         
Since the parameter cube is a compact metric space and the empirical risk is Borel measurable in the training sample and continuous in the parameter (hence Carath\'eodory), the measurable maximum theorem \citep[Theorem~18.19]{aliprantisborder2006}, applied to the negative empirical risk, yields a Borel-measurable parameter selector whose induced score is an exact empirical-risk minimizer; see also \citet{brownpurves1973}.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:balanced-clipping-bias}
For every $R_{\mathrm{lab}} > 0$,
\begin{equation}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}})
  \le A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}(\|\xi\|_2-R_{\mathrm{lab}})_{+}^2,
  \qquad \xi \sim {\mathcal{N}}(0, I_{d_z}).
  \label{eq:balanced-clipping-bias-gaussian}
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every $x \ge 0$, taking $R_{\mathrm{lab}} \ge \sqrt{d_z}+x$ gives
\begin{equation}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}})
  \le 2A_{t_0, T}e^{-x^2/2}.
  \label{eq:balanced-clipping-bias}
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let $\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}:={\mathrm{clip}}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}(-\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}})$.
By \cref{prop:balanced-dsm-identity}, $\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_{\tau},\tau\,|\, H_n)={\mathbb{E}}[-\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}}\,|\, Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n]$, whereas $\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n)={\mathbb{E}}[\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}\,|\, Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n]$.
Conditional Jensen's inequality therefore gives
\begin{align*}
  \left\|\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}
  (Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n)-\widetilde{s}^{\star}(Z_{\tau},\tau\,|\, H_n)
  \right\|_2^2
  &\le
  {\mathbb{E}}\!\left[
  \|\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}
  +\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}}\|_2^2 \,|\, Z_{\tau},\tau,H_n\right].
\end{align*}
For radial clipping, $\|{\mathrm{clip}}_R(u)-u\|_2 = (\|u\|_2-R)_{+}$.
Hence
\begin{equation*}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}})
  \le A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}(\|\xi_{\mathrm{dsm}}\|_2-R_{\mathrm{lab}})_{+}^2,
\end{equation*}
which proves \cref{eq:balanced-clipping-bias-gaussian}.
The Gaussian tail moment bound \cref{eq:gaussian-radial-tail-moment}, with $R=\sqrt{d_z}+x$, proves \cref{eq:balanced-clipping-bias}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:balanced-score-oracle}
Fix $N \ge 1$, $R_{\mathrm{lab}} > 0$, $0 \le R_{\mathrm{pred}}<\infty$, and $0\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}\le R_{\mathrm{pred}}+R_{\mathrm{lab}}$.
Let ${\mathcal{D}}_N^{\mathrm{dsm}}$ be the i.i.d.\ augmented training sample in \cref{eq:dsm-augmented-iid,eq:dsm-training-sample}, and let ${\mathcal{S}}$ be a nonempty deterministic class of jointly Borel scores satisfying
\begin{align}
  \sup_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\sup_{z,t,h}\|\sigma_t s(z,t,h)\|_2
  &\le R_{\mathrm{pred}},
  \nonumber\\
  N_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathcal{S}},
  d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty})&<\infty.
  \label{eq:score-class-envelope}
\end{align}
Let $\widehat{s}_N \in {\mathcal{S}}$ be a jointly measurable approximate ERM satisfying \cref{eq:approximate-erm} with deterministic optimization error $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}$.
For every $\delta \in (0, 1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$ over ${\mathcal{D}}_N^{\mathrm{dsm}}$,
\begin{align}
  {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(\widehat{s}_N)
  &\lesssim
