  {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{app}}({\mathcal{S}})
  +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}})
  +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}
  \nonumber\\
  &\quad+A_{t_0, T}(R_{\mathrm{pred}}+R_{\mathrm{lab}})\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}
  \nonumber\\
  &\quad+A_{t_0, T}(R_{\mathrm{pred}}+R_{\mathrm{lab}})^2
  \frac{\log\!\bigl(2N_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},
  {\mathcal{S}},d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty})/\delta\bigr)}{N}.
  \label{eq:balanced-score-oracle}
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
We first show that the excess loss has variance controlled by its expectation.
This gives a Bernstein bound on a finite score cover, which we extend to the entire score class before applying the approximate-ERM inequality and converting the clipped regression error to score error.

The clipped regression function satisfies
\begin{equation*}
  \widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)
  = {\mathbb{E}}[\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}\,|\, Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n],
  \qquad
  \|\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)\|_2
  \le R_{\mathrm{lab}}
\end{equation*}
almost surely.
For $s \in {\mathcal{S}}$, define its clipped excess loss by
\begin{align*}
  L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}
  &:= \|\widetilde{s}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)-\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}\|_2^2
  -\|\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)-\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}\|_2^2,
  \\
  {\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  &= {\mathbb{E}}\|\widetilde{s}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)
  -\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)\|_2^2.
\end{align*}
The second identity is the regression Pythagorean identity, and all expectations below are over one episode augmented for variance-balanced DSM.
For brevity, put
\begin{equation*}
  R_{\mathrm{loss}} := R_{\mathrm{pred}}+R_{\mathrm{lab}},
  \qquad
  \Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)
  := \log\!\bigl(2N_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},{\mathcal{S}},d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty})
  /\delta\bigr).
\end{equation*}
The difference of squared losses factors as
\begin{equation*}
  L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}
  = \left\langle
  \widetilde{s}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)
  -\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n),
  \widetilde{s}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)
  +\widetilde{s}_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{lab}}(Z_{\tau}, \tau, H_n)
  -2\xi_{R_{\mathrm{lab}}}^{\mathrm{clip}}
  \right\rangle.
\end{equation*}
The first factor is the prediction error relative to the clipped regression function; the bounds on the scaled scores and clipped labels control the second factor.
Together with the regression identity above, this gives
\begin{equation*}
  |L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}| \le 3R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2,
  \qquad
  {\mathbb{E}}[(L_s^{\mathrm{exc}})^2]
  \le 9R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}].
\end{equation*}
In particular, $|L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}-{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]| \le 6R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2$ and $\mathrm{Var}(L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}) \le 9R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]$.
Thus the excess losses obey the variance--expectation condition underlying standard fast-rate regression bounds \citep{bartlettbousquetmendelson2005,koltchinskii2011oracle}.
Here a finite-cover argument suffices; we do not invoke a theorem based on local Rademacher complexity for the nonconvex Transformer class.
Under the convention in \cref{app:sec:transformer-approximation} that cover centers belong to the score class, let ${\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}})\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}$ be a minimum-cardinality $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}$-net in $d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty}$.
This net is used only in the proof; empirical minimization remains over the entire score class ${\mathcal{S}}$.
Let $\widehat{{\mathbb{E}}}_N$ denote the empirical average over the $N$ episodes augmented for variance-balanced DSM.
Bernstein's inequality \citep{boucheron2013concentration} and a union bound imply that, with probability at least $1-\delta$, simultaneously for every $u \in {\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}})$,
\begin{equation}
  |\widehat{{\mathbb{E}}}_N[L_u^{\mathrm{exc}}]-{\mathbb{E}}[L_u^{\mathrm{exc}}]|
  \le
  \sqrt{\frac{18R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2{\mathbb{E}}[L_u^{\mathrm{exc}}]\Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)}{N}}
  +\frac{4R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2\Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)}{N}.
  \label{eq:appendix-bernstein-two-sided}
\end{equation}
For every $s \in {\mathcal{S}}$, choose $s^{\sharp} \in {\mathcal{S}}_{\mathrm{cov}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}})$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
  \|\sigma_t s-\sigma_t s^{\sharp}\|_{\infty,2}
  \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}.
\end{equation*}
The difference of squares and the bounds on the scaled score maps and clipped labels give
\begin{equation}
  |L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}-L_{s^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{exc}}|
  \le 2R_{\mathrm{loss}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},
  \qquad
  {\mathbb{E}}[L_{s^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  \le 2{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]+2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}^2.
  \label{eq:appendix-cover-transfer}
\end{equation}
The first inequality controls both its empirical and population averages.
Combining \cref{eq:appendix-bernstein-two-sided,eq:appendix-cover-transfer}, using $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}} \le R_{\mathrm{loss}}$, and applying $ab \le (a^2+b^2)/2$ to the resulting cross term yields, on the same event,
\begin{equation}
  |\widehat{{\mathbb{E}}}_N[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]-{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]|
  \lesssim
  \sqrt{\frac{R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]\Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)}{N}}
  +\frac{R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2\Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)}{N}
  +R_{\mathrm{loss}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}},
  \qquad s \in {\mathcal{S}}.
  \label{eq:appendix-full-class-bernstein}
\end{equation}

For any deterministic $s\in{\mathcal{S}}$, subtract the empirical loss of the clipped regression function from \cref{eq:approximate-erm}.
Apply \cref{eq:appendix-full-class-bernstein} to both $\widehat{s}_N$ and $s$ on its simultaneous event.
Young's inequality absorbs the square-root term involving ${\mathbb{E}}[L_{\widehat{s}_N}^{\mathrm{exc}}]$ into the left side and bounds the comparator's square-root term by its risk plus the complexity term.
Multiplying by $A_{t_0,T}$ yields
\begin{equation*}
  A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}[L_{\widehat{s}_N}^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  \lesssim
  A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  +A_{t_0, T}R_{\mathrm{loss}}^2\frac{\Gamma_{\mathrm{cov}}(\delta)}{N}
  +A_{t_0, T}R_{\mathrm{loss}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cov}}
  +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}.
\end{equation*}
For every $s \in {\mathcal{S}}$, the squared triangle inequality and the inverse-variance time distribution give
\begin{equation*}
  A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}[L_s^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  \le 2{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(s)
  +2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}}),
\end{equation*}
while \cref{eq:clipped-to-exact-score} gives
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(\widehat{s}_N)
  \le 2A_{t_0, T}{\mathbb{E}}[L_{\widehat{s}_N}^{\mathrm{exc}}]
  +2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab}}).
\end{equation*}
By the convention of \cref{app:subsec:episodic-learning}, for each realized training sample this population error is exactly the conditional error on a fresh episode.
Combining the preceding bounds and taking the infimum over $s \in {\mathcal{S}}$ proves \cref{eq:balanced-score-oracle} with a universal implicit constant.
Every concentration variable above is one complete episode augmented for variance-balanced DSM; auxiliary perturbations of the same episode do not increase the independent sample size beyond $N$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:cube-holder-tv-stopping}
Let $P({\mathrm{d}} z) = p(z) \,{\mathrm{d}} z$ on ${\mathcal{Z}} = [0, 1]^{d_z}$ with $p \in {\mathcal{F}}_{\beta}^{d_z}(L_{\beta})$, and let $P_t$ be its OU evolution in \cref{eq:forward-ou-fixed-time}.
There is $C_{\mathrm{stop}} = C_{\mathrm{stop}}(\beta, d_z, L_{\beta}) < \infty$ such that, for every $0 < t \le 1$,
\begin{align*}
  {\mathsf{TV}}\left((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P_t, P\right)
  &\le {\mathsf{TV}}(P_t, P)
  \le C_{\mathrm{stop}}t^{\beta'/2}.
\end{align*}
No lower density bound is required.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Write $d=d_z$, and let $\bar{p}$ be the zero extension of $p$ from ${\mathcal{Z}}=[0,1]^d$ to ${\mathbb{R}}^d$.
First note the global translation modulus
\begin{equation}
  \|\bar{p}(\cdot-y)-\bar{p}\|_{L^1({\mathbb{R}}^d)}
  \le H_{\mathrm{tr}}\|y\|_2^{\beta'},
  \qquad y \in {\mathbb{R}}^d,
  \label{eq:appendix-zero-extension-translation}
\end{equation}
for a constant $H_{\mathrm{tr}}=H_{\mathrm{tr}}(d,\beta,L_{\beta})$.
Indeed, on ${\mathcal{Z}}\cap({\mathcal{Z}}+y)$ the H\"older condition gives a bound of order $\|y\|_2^{\beta'}$ (the bounded gradient gives power one when $\beta>1$), while the two boundary layers have Lebesgue measure at most $C_d\min\{1,\|y\|_2\}$.
Since $\|p\|_{\infty}\le L_{\beta}$, their contribution has the same power for $\|y\|_2\le1$; for larger translations the trivial $L^1$ bound by two proves \cref{eq:appendix-zero-extension-translation} after increasing $H_{\mathrm{tr}}$.

For $0 < \alpha \le 1$, set $D_{\alpha}\bar{p}(x) := \alpha^{-d}\bar{p}(x/\alpha)$.
Uniformly for $e^{-1} \le \alpha \le 1$,
\begin{equation}
  \|D_{\alpha}\bar{p}-\bar{p}\|_1
  \le H_{\mathrm{dil}}(1-\alpha)^{\beta'},
  \label{eq:appendix-zero-extension-dilation}
\end{equation}
for a constant $H_{\mathrm{dil}}=H_{\mathrm{dil}}(d,\beta,L_{\beta})$.
To see this, split the integral over $\alpha{\mathcal{Z}}$ and ${\mathcal{Z}}\setminus\alpha{\mathcal{Z}}$.
The normalization difference and the boundary layer are ${\mathcal{O}}(1-\alpha)$.
On $\alpha{\mathcal{Z}}$ compare $p(x/\alpha)$ with $p(x)$; their arguments are at distance at most $\sqrt{d}(\alpha^{-1}-1)$, so H\"older continuity (or the bounded gradient) gives ${\mathcal{O}}((1-\alpha)^{\beta'})$.
This proves \cref{eq:appendix-zero-extension-dilation}.
A change of variables in \cref{eq:appendix-zero-extension-translation} also gives
\begin{equation}
  \|D_{\alpha}\bar{p}(\cdot-y)-D_{\alpha}\bar{p}\|_1
  \le H_{\mathrm{tr}}\alpha^{-\beta'}\|y\|_2^{\beta'}.
  \label{eq:appendix-dilated-translation}
\end{equation}

Put $\alpha = \alpha_t = e^{-t}$ and $\sigma = \sigma_t$.
Let $\varphi_{\sigma}^{(d)}$ be the centered $d$-variate Gaussian density with covariance $\sigma^2 I_d$.
The OU density is $p_t = \varphi_{\sigma}^{(d)}*D_{\alpha}\bar{p}$.
Minkowski's inequality, \cref{eq:appendix-zero-extension-dilation,eq:appendix-dilated-translation}, and $\xi \sim {\mathcal{N}}(0, I_d)$ yield
\begin{align}
  \|p_t-\bar{p}\|_1
  &\le
  H_{\mathrm{tr}}\alpha^{-\beta'}\sigma^{\beta'}{\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi\|_2^{\beta'}]
  +H_{\mathrm{dil}}(1-\alpha)^{\beta'}.
  \label{eq:appendix-ou-holder-l1}
\end{align}
For $0 < t \le 1$, $\alpha^{-\beta'} \le e$, $\sigma \le \sqrt{2t}$, $1-\alpha \le t$, and ${\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi\|_2^{\beta'}] \le d^{\beta'/2}$.
Dividing \cref{eq:appendix-ou-holder-l1} by two gives ${\mathsf{TV}}(P_t, P) \le C_{\mathrm{stop}}t^{\beta'/2}$.
Finally, projection is a Markov kernel and fixes every point of ${\mathcal{Z}}$, so
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathsf{TV}}((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P_t, P)
  = {\mathsf{TV}}((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P_t, (\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P)
  \le {\mathsf{TV}}(P_t, P).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:conditional-tv-stopping}
Fix $\beta>0$ and $L_\beta\ge1$, and consider either the sample-only or the input--output prompt model in \cref{eq:hierarchical-sample,eq:input-output-episode} with any Borel task prior $\Pi$.
Suppose the task laws admit jointly Borel densities $p_\theta(z)$ in the sample-only model or $f_\theta(x,z)$ in the input--output model, with $p_\Theta\in{\mathcal{F}}_\beta^{d_z}(L_\beta)$ or $f_\Theta\in{\mathcal{F}}_\beta^{d_x+d_z}(L_\beta)$, respectively, $\Pi$-almost surely.
Writing $\beta'=\beta\wedge1$, there is a constant $C_{\mathrm{stop}}$ depending only on $(\beta,L_\beta)$ and the dimensions such that
\begin{equation}
  {\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathsf{TV}}\left(
  (\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P_{t \,|\, H_n}, P_{0 \,|\, H_n}
  \right)\right]
  \le C_{\mathrm{stop}}t^{\beta'/2},
  \qquad 0 < t \le 1.
  \label{eq:posterior-predictive-tv-stopping}
\end{equation}
For any standard Borel space $\mathcal{U}$ and Borel map $\phi_{\mathrm{pr}}:{\mathcal{H}}_n\to\mathcal{U}$, set $U=\phi_{\mathrm{pr}}(H_n)$.
Write $P_{0\mid U}:=\mathsf{Law}(Z_0\mid U)$ and $P_{t\mid U}:=\mathsf{Law}(Z_t\mid U)$.
The same bound holds with $H_n$ replaced by $U$.
In the input--output model, the expectation is over the query drawn from its input marginal; no lower bound on its marginal density is assumed.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
In the sample-only task family, for almost every prompt $h$, the density of $P_{0 \,|\, h}$ is the posterior mixture of the task densities.
The H\"older ball is convex, so \cref{lem:cube-holder-tv-stopping} applies with the same constant and averaging proves \cref{eq:posterior-predictive-tv-stopping}.

For the input--output task family, condition first on the demonstrations $C_n = c$.
Let
\begin{equation*}
  f_c(x,z):=\int f_{\theta}(x,z)\Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta\,|\, C_n=c),
  \qquad
  \lambda_c(x):=\int_{{\mathcal{Z}}} f_c(x,z)\,{\mathrm{d}} z.
\end{equation*}
The query has density $\lambda_c$, and where $\lambda_c(x)>0$ the predictive output density is $f_c(x,\cdot)/\lambda_c(x)$.
Let $\mathsf{T}_t^z$ denote unprojected OU evolution in the $z$ coordinate and extend $f_c(x, \cdot)$ by zero outside the cube.
Projection contraction, linearity, and cancellation of the query marginal give
\begin{align*}
 {\mathbb{E}}\!\left[
 {\mathsf{TV}}((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_{\#}P_{t \,|\, H_n}, P_{0 \,|\, H_n})
 \,|\, C_n = c\right]
 \le
 \frac{1}{2}\int_{[0, 1]^{d_x}}
 \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d_z}}
 \left|\mathsf{T}_t^z f_c(x, z)-f_c(x, z)\right|{\mathrm{d}} z \,{\mathrm{d}} x.
\end{align*}
The proof of \cref{lem:cube-holder-tv-stopping} applies verbatim to each nonnegative subdensity $f_c(x, \cdot)$: its mass and its $z$-H\"older norm are bounded uniformly by a constant depending only on $L_{\beta}$.
Integrating over the unit-volume input cube proves the asserted bound without dividing by a lower bound for $\lambda_c$.

For $U=\phi_{\mathrm{pr}}(H_n)$, both conditional laws are mixtures of their counterparts given $H_n$.
Convexity of TV and the tower property give
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_\#P_{t\mid U},P_{0\mid U})
 \le{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}((\mathrm{Proj}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})_\#P_{t\mid H_n},P_{0\mid H_n}),
\end{equation*}
proving the claim with conditioning on $U$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:finite-energy-girsanov}
Fix $d\in\mathbb{N}$, a finite path horizon $T_{\mathrm{path}}>0$, and an initial distribution $\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^d)$.
Let $\mathsf{Q}$ be the law on $C([0,T_{\mathrm{path}}];{\mathbb{R}}^d)$ of
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathrm{d}} Y_r = b(r, Y_r) \,{\mathrm{d}} r+\sqrt{2}\,{\mathrm{d}} W_r,
  \qquad Y_0 \sim \mu,
\end{equation*}
where $b:[0,T_{\mathrm{path}}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^d\to{\mathbb{R}}^d$ is Borel, globally Lipschitz in its state variable uniformly in $r$, and has linear growth.
Let $b^{\star}:[0,T_{\mathrm{path}}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^d\to{\mathbb{R}}^d$ be Borel, and let $\mathsf{P}^{\star}$ be the law of a weak solution with the same initial distribution and drift $b^{\star}$.
If
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}^{\star}}\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{path}}}
  \|b^{\star}(r, Y_r)-b(r, Y_r)\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r < \infty,
\end{equation*}
then
\begin{equation}
  \mathrm{KL}(\mathsf{P}^{\star}\|\mathsf{Q})
  \le \frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}^{\star}}\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{path}}}
  \|b^{\star}(r, Y_r)-b(r, Y_r)\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r.
  \label{eq:finite-energy-girsanov}
\end{equation}
The same conclusion holds when the reference process is defined by a recursively frozen nonanticipative drift.
Specifically, choose a deterministic partition $0=r_0<\cdots<r_K=T_{\mathrm{path}}$ and finite-valued Borel maps $a_k:{\mathbb{R}}^d\to{\mathbb{R}}^d$, $0\le k<K$, and set
\begin{equation*}
  b_{\mathrm{fr}}(r,\omega)
  :=\omega_r+2a_k(\omega_{r_k}),
  \qquad r\in[r_k,r_{k+1}),
\end{equation*}
with the last interval closed at $T_{\mathrm{path}}$.
In this alternative case, let $\mathsf{Q}$ denote the law of the unique interval-by-interval solution of ${\mathrm{d}} Y_r=b_{\mathrm{fr}}(r,Y_{[0,r]})\,{\mathrm{d}} r+\sqrt{2}\,{\mathrm{d}} W_r$ with $Y_0\sim\mu$.
In this case, replace $b(r,Y_r)$ in the energy condition and in \cref{eq:finite-energy-girsanov} by $b_{\mathrm{fr}}(r,Y_{[0,r]})$.
The construction includes $a_k(x)=\widehat{s}(x,\vartheta_k,h)$ for fixed score-evaluation times $\vartheta_k$ whenever $\widehat{s}$ is Borel and finite-valued.
The reference process and every post-stopping continuation are then constructed explicitly, interval by interval, so no state regularity of the maps $a_k$ is required.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Put $\Delta b_r=b^{\star}(r,Y_r)-b(r,Y_r)$ in the Markov case and $\Delta b_r=b^{\star}(r,Y_r)-b_{\mathrm{fr}}(r,Y_{[0,r]})$ in the recursively frozen case, and define
\begin{equation*}
  \upsilon_m := \inf\left\{r:\int_0^r\|\Delta b_u\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} u \ge m\right\}
  \wedge T_{\mathrm{path}}.
\end{equation*}
On the filtered weak-solution space carrying $\mathsf{P}^{\star}$, the process $\Delta b$ is progressively measurable, so $\upsilon_m$ is a stopping time.
On a realization of the weak solution under $\mathsf{P}^{\star}$, retain its path through $\upsilon_m$ and, after $\upsilon_m$, continue with the reference drift and the same Brownian increments.
Global Lipschitzness of $b$ makes this continuation well defined in the Markov case; the recursively frozen case is constructed successively on the remaining grid intervals.
Indeed, conditional on the left endpoint of an interval $[r_k,r_{k+1}]$, its frozen map value is finite and measurable, and the resulting affine SDE has a unique explicit solution.
Induction over the deterministic partition gives weak uniqueness for the recursively frozen reference process and for every post-stopping continuation.
Denote its law by $\mathsf{P}_m$.
The coupling gives
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathsf{TV}}(\mathsf{P}_m, \mathsf{P}^{\star})
  \le \mathsf{P}^{\star}(\upsilon_m < T_{\mathrm{path}})
  \le \frac{1}{m}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}^{\star}}\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{path}}}\|\Delta b_r\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r
  \longrightarrow 0.
\end{equation*}
Relative to $\mathsf{Q}$, the drift change under $\mathsf{P}_m$ is $\operatorname{\mathds{1}}\{r\le\upsilon_m\}\Delta b_r$.
Its accumulated squared norm is at most $m$.
Hence Novikov's criterion holds because
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}_m}\exp\!\left\{
  \frac{1}{4}\int_0^{\upsilon_m}\|\Delta b_r\|_2^2\,{\mathrm{d}} r
  \right\}
  \le e^{m/4}<\infty.
\end{equation*}
If $W^{(m)}$ denotes the Brownian motion driving the process obtained by joining the two segments, define the stochastic exponential
\begin{equation*}
  D_u^{(m)}
  := \exp\left\{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int_0^{u\wedge\upsilon_m}
  \Delta b_r^{\top} \,{\mathrm{d}} W_r^{(m)}
  -\frac{1}{4}\int_0^{u\wedge\upsilon_m}\|\Delta b_r\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r\right\},
  \qquad 0\le u\le T_{\mathrm{path}}.
\end{equation*}
This process is therefore a true, uniformly integrable martingale of mean one.
Define $\mathsf{Q}_m$ by ${\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{Q}_m/{\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{P}_m=D_{T_{\mathrm{path}}}^{(m)}$. Girsanov's theorem then identifies its drift.
Under $\mathsf{Q}_m$ the coordinate process has the reference drift $b$ in the Markov case and $b_{\mathrm{fr}}$ in the recursively frozen case.
Weak uniqueness for the globally Lipschitz reference equation identifies $\mathsf{Q}_m = \mathsf{Q}$ in the Markov case; the interval-by-interval construction gives the same identification in the recursively frozen case.
Taking the $\mathsf{P}_m$-expectation of $\log({\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{P}_m/{\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{Q})$ eliminates the stochastic integral and gives
\begin{equation*}
  \mathrm{KL}(\mathsf{P}_m\|\mathsf{Q})
  = \frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}_m}
  \int_0^{\upsilon_m}\|\Delta b_r\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r
  = \frac{1}{4}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}^{\star}}
  \int_0^{\upsilon_m}\|\Delta b_r\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r.
\end{equation*}
Lower semicontinuity of relative entropy under $\mathsf{P}_m \to \mathsf{P}^{\star}$ in total variation, followed by monotone convergence, proves \cref{eq:finite-energy-girsanov}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:exact-reverse-score-dynamics}
Fix a prompt $h$ for which $P_{0\,|\, h}$ is supported on $[0,1]^{d_z}$, and let $p_t(\cdot\,|\, h)$ and $s_t^{\star}(\cdot\,|\, h)=\nabla\log p_t(\cdot\,|\, h)$ be its OU marginal density and score.
For $0<a<b<\infty$, let $(Y_r)_{0\le r\le b-a}$ be the exact reverse process
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathrm{d}} Y_r
  =\{Y_r+2s_{b-r}^{\star}(Y_r\,|\, h)\}\,{\mathrm{d}} r
  +\sqrt{2}\,{\mathrm{d}} W_r,
  \qquad
  Y_0\sim P_{b\,|\, h}.
\end{equation*}
Then $Y_r\sim P_{b-r\,|\, h}$, and the forward representation $Z_t=\alpha_tZ_0+\sigma_t\xi$ gives
\begin{equation}
  s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)
  = -\sigma_t^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}[\xi \,|\, Z_t, h],
  \qquad
  \nabla s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)
  = \sigma_t^{-2}\{\mathrm{Cov}(\xi \,|\, Z_t, h)-I_{d_z}\}.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-identities}
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{align}
  {\mathbb{E}}\|s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)\|_2^2
  &\le \frac{d_z}{\sigma_t^2},
  \nonumber\\
  {\mathbb{E}}\|\nabla s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2
  &\le \frac{d_z(d_z+3)}{\sigma_t^4}.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-fisher-hessian}
\end{align}
Moreover, $S_r:=s_{b-r}^{\star}(Y_r\,|\, h)$ is a continuous semimartingale satisfying
\begin{equation}
  {\mathrm{d}} S_r
  =-S_r\,{\mathrm{d}} r
  +\sqrt{2}\,\nabla s_{b-r}^{\star}(Y_r\,|\, h)\,{\mathrm{d}} W_r.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-process}
\end{equation}
All identities hold on the full interval $0\le r\le b-a$ in the usual localized It\^o sense.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
At every positive time, Gaussian convolution makes $p_t(\cdot\,|\, h)$ strictly positive and smooth.
Gaussian differentiation gives \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-identities}.
Conditional Jensen proves the score bound.
For $\Sigma_{t,h}:=\mathrm{Cov}(\xi\,|\, Z_t,h)$, one has $\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t,h}^2)\le\{\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t,h})\}^2$ and
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}\{\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t,h})\}^2
  \le {\mathbb{E}}\|\xi\|_2^4
  =d_z(d_z+2).
\end{equation*}
Since $\|\Sigma_{t,h}-I_{d_z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2\le\{\mathrm{Tr}(\Sigma_{t,h})\}^2+d_z$, this proves the Hessian bound.
The positive-time diffusion time-reversal identity gives $Y_r\sim P_{b-r\,|\, h}$.
Stop the process when it leaves a compact state set and apply It\^o's formula to $(r,y)\mapsto s_{b-r}^{\star}(y\,|\, h)$.
Differentiating the OU Fokker--Planck equation gives
\begin{equation*}
  \partial_t s_t^{\star}
  =s_t^{\star}+(\nabla s_t^{\star})y
  +\nabla\operatorname{div}s_t^{\star}
  +2(\nabla s_t^{\star})s_t^{\star}.
\end{equation*}
Substitution of the exact reverse drift cancels all finite-variation terms except $-S_r\,{\mathrm{d}} r$, proving \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-process} up to the stopping time.
The moment bounds in \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-fisher-hessian}, together with $b-r\ge a$, give the square integrability required to let the localization radii tend to infinity and obtain the identity on the full interval.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:randomized-grid-discretization}
Consider the randomized time-grid sampler in \cref{app:subsec:randomized-grid-sampler}.
Let $\widehat{s}$ be trained independently of the test episode.
Conditionally on the completed training run, assume that $\widehat{s}$ is finite-valued and jointly Borel and that ${\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(\widehat{s})<\infty$.
Then
\begin{align}
  &{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\bigl(
  \widehat{Q}_{t_0 \,|\, H_n}^{\mathrm{grid},h_{\mathrm{grid}}},
  P_{t_0 \,|\, H_n}\bigr)
  \le
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{init}}(T-h_{\mathrm{grid}})
  +\sqrt{\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(\widehat{s})}{2}}
  +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grid}}(h_{\mathrm{grid}}),
  \label{eq:randomized-grid-discretization-bound}\\
  &\varepsilon_{\mathrm{grid}}(h_{\mathrm{grid}})
  :=
  \left\{
    \frac{d_z(T-t_0)h_{\mathrm{grid}}^2}{6}
    +d_z(d_z+3)e^{2h_{\mathrm{grid}}}h_{\mathrm{grid}} A_{t_0,T}
  \right\}^{1/2},
  \quad h_{\mathrm{grid}}>0.
  \label{eq:randomized-grid-discretization-error}
\end{align}
The number of reverse updates is at most $\lceil A_{t_0,T}/h_{\mathrm{grid}}\rceil$ almost surely.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
We first compare the exact reverse process with the process that uses the same initialization and freezes the score on each time-grid cell.
The score difference splits into variation of the exact score within each cell and learned-score error at the grid nodes; averaging over the random shift controls the latter by the integrated score error.
The finite-energy KL bound then gives a TV bound, after which we account for Gaussian initialization.

Condition on the learned score and fix a fresh prompt $h$ in a common full-measure set on which the conditional integrated squared score error is finite.

To bound the variation of the exact score, consider one time-grid cell.
Let $Y_r$ be the exact reverse path initialized at its upper forward time and write $S_r = s_{v_j-r}^{\star}(Y_r \,|\, h)$ for $0 \le r \le \Delta_j = v_j-v_{j-1}$.
Define the integrated squared error from freezing the exact score within each time-grid cell by
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{F}_h(U_{\mathrm{grid}})
  :=\sum_{j=0}^{J_{\mathrm{grid}}}
  \int_0^{\Delta_j}{\mathbb{E}}\|S_r-S_0\|_2^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r.
\end{equation*}
Applying \cref{lem:exact-reverse-score-dynamics} with $(a,b)=(v_{j-1},v_j)$ gives \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-fisher-hessian,eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-process} on this cell.

The time grid satisfies
\begin{equation*}
  \frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{A}_{t_0}(t)}\log\sigma_t^2
  = 2e^{-2t} \le 2.
\end{equation*}
Hence, whenever $t \in [v_{j-1}, v_j]$,
\begin{equation}
  \Delta_j \le h_{\mathrm{grid}}\sigma_{v_j}^2
  \le h_{\mathrm{grid}}e^{2h_{\mathrm{grid}}}
  \sigma_t^2,
  \qquad
  \Delta_j \le h_{\mathrm{grid}}.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-cell-length}
\end{equation}
Solving \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-score-process} on the cell gives
\begin{equation*}
  S_r-S_0 = (e^{-r}-1)S_0
  +\sqrt{2}\int_0^re^{-(r-u)}
  \nabla s_{v_j-u}^{\star}(Y_u \,|\, h) \,{\mathrm{d}} W_u.
\end{equation*}
The two terms are orthogonal in $L^2$.
Since $\int_0^{\Delta_j}(1-e^{-r})^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} r \le \Delta_j^3/3$, ${\mathbb{E}}\|S_0\|_2^2 \le d_z/\sigma_{v_j}^2$, and $\Delta_j/\sigma_{v_j}^2 \le h_{\mathrm{grid}}$, the summed drift contribution is at most $d_z(T-t_0)h_{\mathrm{grid}}^2/3$.
It\^o isometry bounds the summed martingale contribution by
\begin{equation*}
  2\sum_j\Delta_j\int_{v_{j-1}}^{v_j}
  {\mathbb{E}}\|\nabla s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)\|_{\mathrm{F}}^2 \,{\mathrm{d}} t.
\end{equation*}
Use $\Delta_j/\sigma_t^2 \le h_{\mathrm{grid}}e^{2h_{\mathrm{grid}}}$ and \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-fisher-hessian}.
Summing the cells gives
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{F}_h(U_{\mathrm{grid}})
  \le \frac{d_z}{3}(T-t_0)h_{\mathrm{grid}}^2
  +2d_z(d_z+3)e^{2h_{\mathrm{grid}}}
  h_{\mathrm{grid}}A_{t_0,T}.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-freezing-energy}
\end{equation}
This bound is uniform in $h$ and $U_{\mathrm{grid}}$.

We next control the learned-score error at the grid nodes.
Since the learned score is only measurable, we use averaging over the random shift rather than a time-continuity bound.
Put
\begin{equation*}
  e_h(t) := {\mathbb{E}}_{P_{t \,|\, h}}
  \|s_t^{\star}(Z_t \,|\, h)-\widehat{s}(Z_t, t, h)\|_2^2.
\end{equation*}
Joint Borel measurability of the scores and the conditional OU kernel makes $e_h$ Lebesgue measurable, and the finite conditional integrated squared score error makes it integrable.
A random grid shift hits any null set of exceptional times with probability zero; the countable union over lattice indices is still null.
By \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-cell-length}, the learned-score grid contribution is at most
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{E}_h^{\mathrm{grid}}(U_{\mathrm{grid}})
  := h_{\mathrm{grid}}
  \sum_{j = 0}^{J_{\mathrm{grid}}}\sigma_{v_j}^2e_h(v_j).
\end{equation*}
Averaging the shifted lattice over $U_{\mathrm{grid}}$ and applying the change of variables $x = \mathsf{A}_{t_0}(t)$ gives exactly
\begin{align}
  {\mathbb{E}}_{U_{\mathrm{grid}}}\mathcal{E}_h^{\mathrm{grid}}(U_{\mathrm{grid}})
  &= \int_0^{A_{t_0,T}}\sigma_{\mathsf{A}_{t_0}^{-1}(x)}^2
  e_h\{\mathsf{A}_{t_0}^{-1}(x)\} \,{\mathrm{d}} x
  = \int_{t_0}^Te_h(t) \,{\mathrm{d}} t.
  \label{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-quadrature}
\end{align}

The two integrated squared-error bounds now control the difference between the exact and frozen reverse drifts.
For almost every grid shift $u$, let $\mathsf{P}_{h,u}^{\star}$ be the exact reverse path law initialized from $P_{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{grid}}(u)\,|\, h}$, and let $\mathsf{Q}_{h,u}$ be the equally initialized recursively frozen path law.
Represent both paths in the decreasing forward-time coordinate $t\in[t_0,\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{grid}}(u)]$, write $Z_t^{\leftarrow}$ for the coordinate process under $\mathsf{P}_{h,u}^{\star}$, and let $\kappa_u(t)=v_j$ for almost every $t\in[v_{j-1},v_j]$.
Define the integrated squared score error along the full reverse path
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{I}_h(u)
  :=\int_{t_0}^{\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{grid}}(u)}
  {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathsf{P}_{h,u}^{\star}}\!\left[
  \left\|s_t^{\star}(Z_t^{\leftarrow}\,|\, h)
  -\widehat{s}(Z_{\kappa_u(t)}^{\leftarrow},\kappa_u(t),h)\right\|_2^2
  \right] \,{\mathrm{d}} t.
\end{equation*}
The reference process is defined interval by interval by the explicit exponential--Euler formula, so finite-valued Borel evaluation of $\widehat{s}$ suffices to construct it with the score frozen on each interval.
The reverse-drift difference is twice the score difference defining $\mathcal{I}_h(u)$, so the factor $1/4$ in \cref{eq:finite-energy-girsanov} gives the first inequality below.
Minkowski's inequality with respect to time and the exact reverse-path law, followed by \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-cell-length}, gives the remaining inequalities:
\begin{align*}
  \mathrm{KL}\!\left(\mathsf{P}_{h,u}^{\star}\|\mathsf{Q}_{h,u}\right)
  &\le \mathcal{I}_h(u),
  \\
  \mathcal{I}_h(u)^{1/2}
  &\le \mathcal{F}_h(u)^{1/2}
  +\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{J_{\mathrm{grid}}}\Delta_j e_h(v_j)\right\}^{1/2}
  \\
  &\le \mathcal{F}_h(u)^{1/2}+\mathcal{E}_h^{\mathrm{grid}}(u)^{1/2}.
\end{align*}
Pinsker's inequality, Jensen's inequality in $U_{\mathrm{grid}}$, \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-freezing-energy}, and \cref{eq:appendix-randomized-grid-quadrature} yield the last two terms in \cref{eq:randomized-grid-discretization-bound} after averaging over $h$.

It remains to replace the exact terminal distribution by the sampler's Gaussian initialization.
Since $A_{t_0,T}-\mathsf{A}_{t_0}(\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{grid}}) < h_{\mathrm{grid}}$ and ${\mathrm{d}} t/{\mathrm{d}}\mathsf{A} = \sigma_t^2 \le 1$, we have $\widetilde{T}_{\mathrm{grid}} \in [T-h_{\mathrm{grid}}, T]$.
Invariance of ${\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d_z})$ under the OU semigroup and TV contraction give the terminal term $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{init}}(T-h_{\mathrm{grid}})$.
Convexity of TV in the first argument permits averaging the output distribution over $U_{\mathrm{grid}}$.
The update count follows because, with $x = A_{t_0,T}/h_{\mathrm{grid}}$ and $y = U_{\mathrm{grid}}/h_{\mathrm{grid}} \in (0, 1)$, $J_{\mathrm{grid}}+1 = \lfloor x-y\rfloor+1 \le \lceil x\rceil$ almost surely.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:randomized-grid-update-bound}
Fix $\beta>0$ and $L_\beta\ge1$, and consider either the sample-only or the input--output prompt model in \cref{eq:hierarchical-sample,eq:input-output-episode} with any Borel task prior $\Pi$.
Suppose the task laws admit jointly Borel densities $p_\theta(z)$ in the sample-only model or $f_\theta(x,z)$ in the input--output model, with $p_\Theta\in{\mathcal{F}}_\beta^{d_z}(L_\beta)$ or $f_\Theta\in{\mathcal{F}}_\beta^{d_x+d_z}(L_\beta)$, respectively, $\Pi$-almost surely.
Let $T>t_0>0$ with $t_0\le1$, and write $\beta'=\beta\wedge1$.
Suppose the learned score is independent of the test episode and, conditionally on the completed training run, finite-valued and jointly Borel with finite integrated squared score error.
Let $M \in \mathbb{N}$ satisfy
\begin{equation*}
  M \ge
  \max\left\{\lceil 2A_{t_0,T}\rceil,
  \left\lceil\frac{A_{t_0,T}}{T-t_0}\right\rceil\right\},
  \qquad
  h_{\mathrm{grid}} := \frac{A_{t_0,T}}{M}.
\end{equation*}
When $T-t_0 \ge 1/2$, as under the parameter choices below for all sufficiently large prompt lengths, the simpler condition $M \ge \lceil 2A_{t_0,T}\rceil$ implies both requirements.
Then the sampler uses at most $M$ reverse updates almost surely, and its output distribution, averaged over the random grid shift, satisfies
\begin{align}
  {\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\left(
  \widehat{P}_{0 \,|\, H_n}^{(M)}, P_{0 \,|\, H_n}\right)
  &\le
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{init}}(T - A_{t_0,T}/M)
  +\sqrt{\frac{{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{score}}(\widehat{s})}{2}}
  +C_{\mathrm{stop}}t_0^{\beta'/2}
  \nonumber\\
  &\qquad
  +\frac{A_{t_0,T}}{M}\sqrt{\frac{d_z(T-t_0)}{6}}
  +e^{A_{t_0,T}/M}\frac{A_{t_0,T}}{\sqrt{M}}\sqrt{d_z(d_z + 3)}.
  \label{eq:randomized-grid-update-bound}
\end{align}
Here $\widehat{P}_{0 \,|\, h}^{(M)}:=\widehat{P}_{0 \,|\, h}^{\mathrm{grid},A_{t_0,T}/M}$, so the parenthesized argument denotes a bound on the number of updates rather than a grid spacing.
Moreover, there is a constant $0<C_{\mathrm{disc}}=C_{\mathrm{disc}}(d_z)<\infty$ such that, for every $0<\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}\le1$, the displayed conditions on $M$ and $M\ge C_{\mathrm{disc}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}^{-2}(A_{t_0,T}+1)^2$ make the sum of the two discretization terms at most $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}$.
Under the parameter choices used in \cref{eq:main-predictive-sufficient-choices}, $A_{t_0,T}\lesssim\log(e/\varepsilon)$ and $\log(e/\varepsilon)\ge1$, so $A_{t_0,T}+1\lesssim\log(e/\varepsilon)\lesssim\log\varepsilon^{-1}$ for $\varepsilon\le1/2$.
Thus, for a sufficiently large implicit constant, the condition on $M$ in \cref{eq:main-predictive-sufficient-choices} implies both inequalities in \cref{eq:admissible-sampling-updates} and the preceding discretization requirement with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}=\varepsilon$.
This is a sufficient number of sampling steps, not a minimax lower bound for the number of reverse updates.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
The conditions on $M$ give $A_{t_0,T}/M \le \min\{1/2, A_{t_0,T}, T-t_0\}$, so \cref{prop:randomized-grid-discretization} applies with $h_{\mathrm{grid}}=A_{t_0,T}/M$.
Projection contracts TV, and the triangle inequality adds the conditional stopping error from \cref{cor:conditional-tv-stopping}.
With $h_{\mathrm{grid}}=A_{t_0,T}/M$, subadditivity of the square root in \cref{eq:randomized-grid-discretization-error} gives
\begin{equation*}
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{grid}}(A_{t_0,T}/M)
  \le
  \frac{A_{t_0,T}}{M}\sqrt{\frac{d_z(T-t_0)}{6}}
  +e^{A_{t_0,T}/M}\frac{A_{t_0,T}}{\sqrt{M}}\sqrt{d_z(d_z+3)}.
\end{equation*}
The update count is $\lceil A_{t_0,T}/(A_{t_0,T}/M)\rceil = M$.
Substitution proves the claim.
Since $\sigma_t^2\le1$, one has $T-t_0\le A_{t_0,T}$, and the condition $M\ge2A_{t_0,T}$ gives $e^{A_{t_0,T}/M}\le e^{1/2}$.
If $M\ge C_{\mathrm{disc}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}^{-2}(A_{t_0,T}+1)^2$, the sum of the two discretization terms is at most
\begin{equation*}
  \frac{\sqrt{d_z/6}}{C_{\mathrm{disc}}}
  \frac{A_{t_0,T}^{3/2}}{(A_{t_0,T}+1)^2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}^2
  +\frac{e^{1/2}\sqrt{d_z(d_z+3)}}{\sqrt{C_{\mathrm{disc}}}}
  \frac{A_{t_0,T}}{A_{t_0,T}+1}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}.
\end{equation*}
Choosing $C_{\mathrm{disc}}(d_z)$ sufficiently large makes this expression at most $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}$ for every $0<\varepsilon_{\mathrm{disc}}\le1$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-latent-explicit-tail-bounds}
Use the diffusion times and radii in \cref{eq:accuracy-diffusion-times,eq:smooth-latent-explicit-radii}, with $\beta'=\beta\wedge1$.
Let $\delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}$ and $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2$ be the probability and score-energy terms in \cref{eq:transformer-good-event-probability,eq:transformer-exceptional-score-energy}.
There is $\varepsilon_1\in(0,1]$, depending only on $d_z$, $\beta'$, $C_{\mathrm{stop}}$, $c_{\mathrm{state}}$, and $c_{\mathrm{lab}}$, such that, for $0<\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_1$ and $c_{\mathrm{state}}\ge2\sqrt{d_z}$, the following bounds hold with implicit constants depending only on the same quantities:
\begin{align}
  \sigma_{t_0}^{-1}
  &\lesssim \varepsilon^{-1/\beta'},
  &
  A
  &\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon),
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-time-prefactors}\\
  R_{\mathrm{bal},n}^2
  &\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-2/\beta'},
  &
  \delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
  &\le \varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2/8},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-radius-probability}\\
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2
  &\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon)
  \varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2/16-2/\beta'},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-energy-rate}\\
  AR_{\mathrm{bal},n}^2
  \delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
  &\lesssim \log^2(e/\varepsilon)
  \varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2/8-2/\beta'},
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-envelope-rate}\\
  \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2
  \{R_{\mathrm{lab},n}\}
  &\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{lab}}^2/2}.
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-label-tail-rate}
\end{align}
Consequently, if
\begin{equation}
  \frac{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2}{16}
  >2+\frac{2}{\beta'},
  \qquad
  \frac{c_{\mathrm{lab}}^2}{2}>2,
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-constant-condition}
\end{equation}
then
\begin{equation}
  4AR_{\mathrm{bal},n}^2
  \delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
  +4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2
  +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2
  \{R_{\mathrm{lab},n}\}
  =o(\varepsilon^2).
  \label{eq:smooth-latent-tail-total-rate}
\end{equation}
In particular, the left-hand side is at most $c_1\varepsilon^2$ for every fixed $c_1>0$ and all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$.
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
For $0<t\le1$, concavity of $t\mapsto1-e^{-2t}$ gives
\begin{equation*}
  \sigma_t^2=1-e^{-2t}\ge(1-e^{-2})t.
\end{equation*}
After decreasing $\varepsilon_1$ so that $t_0\le1$, this proves the first bound in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-time-prefactors}.
Moreover, $e^{2t_0}-1\ge2t_0$ and $e^{2T}-1\le e^{2T}$ imply
\begin{equation*}
  A
  \le T+\frac{1}{2}\log\{1/(2t_0)\}
  \lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon).
\end{equation*}
The definition of $R_{\mathrm{bal},n}$ now gives its bound in \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-radius-probability}.

Let $X:=\|\xi\|_2$ for $\xi\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d_z})$ and put $a:=c_{\mathrm{state}}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}$.
The Gaussian norm concentration inequality gives
\begin{equation*}
  \Pr(X>a)
  \le\exp\{-\tfrac{1}{2}(a-\sqrt{d_z})^2\}
  \le e^{-a^2/8}
  \le\varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2/8},
\end{equation*}
where the second inequality uses $a\ge2\sqrt{d_z}$.
This and \cref{eq:transformer-good-event-probability} prove the probability bound.
Cauchy--Schwarz and the finite fourth Gaussian moment give
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}\!\left[
  (X+2\sqrt{d_z})^2\operatorname{\mathds{1}}\{X>a\}
  \right]
  \le
  \left\{{\mathbb{E}}(X+2\sqrt{d_z})^4\right\}^{1/2}
  \Pr(X>a)^{1/2}
  \lesssim\varepsilon^{c_{\mathrm{state}}^2/16}.
\end{equation*}
Substitution into \cref{eq:derived-score-tail-bound}, together with \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-time-prefactors}, proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-energy-rate}.
Multiplying the bounds for $A$, $R_{\mathrm{bal},n}^2$, and $\delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}$ proves \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-envelope-rate}.
Finally, apply \cref{eq:balanced-clipping-bias} with $x=c_{\mathrm{lab}}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}$ to obtain \cref{eq:smooth-latent-label-tail-rate}.
Under \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-constant-condition}, each exponent remaining after division by $\varepsilon^2$ is strictly positive.
Since $\varepsilon^a\{1+\log(\varepsilon^{-1})\}^k\to0$ for every $a>0$ and fixed $k$, \cref{eq:smooth-latent-tail-total-rate} follows.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:main-generation-error-formal}
Under \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task}, for each $n\ge2$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, there exists a common DiT score class ${\mathcal{S}}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}$ and diffusion parameters that depend on $(n,\varepsilon)$ and the fixed dimensions and regularity bounds. 
Its number of trainable parameters $\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}$, stored nonzero coefficients, and arithmetic operations per forward pass are polynomial in $(n,\varepsilon^{-1})$. Use the estimator and reverse sampler of Section~\ref{subsec:main-training-and-sampling}, with $N\ge1$ independent pretraining episodes, empirical optimization gap $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}\lesssim\varepsilon^2$, and an integer $M$ satisfying \cref{eq:admissible-sampling-updates}. 
For every fixed $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ and $\delta\in(0,1)$, with probability at least $1-\delta$ over the training episodes and their diffusion augmentations,
\begin{equation}
  {\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat P^{(M)}_{0\mid H_n},P(\cdot\mid H_n)\bigr) \lesssim \varepsilon +\log(e/\varepsilon)\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}\log(en/\varepsilon)+\log(2/\delta)}{N}} +\frac{\log(e/\varepsilon)}{\sqrt M}.
\end{equation}
The expectation averages a fresh prompt conditional on the completed training run.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
We first verify approximation and covering for the deterministic class.
We then apply the score-learning inequality and transfer its error to the randomized sampler on the same training event.

The grid and floor choices give \cref{eq:accuracy-grid-size}.
Substitution of \cref{eq:accuracy-component-tolerances} into the definition of $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}$ gives $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{net},n}\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tar},n}/3$.
\Cref{lem:smooth-latent-explicit-tail-bounds,lem:smooth-latent-output-clipping} give
\begin{equation*}
 A R_{\mathrm{bal},n}^2\delta_{\mathrm{tail},n}
 +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tail},n}^2
 +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{out,clip},n}^2
 +\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lab,clip}}^2(R_{\mathrm{lab},n})
 \lesssim\varepsilon^2.
\end{equation*}
In particular the output-clipping contribution is at most $2A\exp(-c_{\mathrm{lab}}^2 \log(e/\varepsilon)/2)=o(\varepsilon^2)$.
Applying \cref{prop:integrated-transformer-approximation} proves
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{app}}({\mathcal{S}}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}})\lesssim\varepsilon^2.
\end{equation*}

The quantitative size and sensitivity bounds in \cref{prop:smooth-latent-complexity} hold for arbitrary grids and component tolerances.
They therefore apply directly to the parameter choices made above for the integrated error.
Here $t_0^{-1}$, $T$, $R_{\mathrm{bal},n}$, the grid size, all inverse nonzero tolerances, and the prior-logit radius are polynomially bounded in $n$ and $\varepsilon^{-1}$.
The local OU moduli and the numbers of components are likewise polynomial.
The fixed-depth attention sensitivity recursion and the clipped readout give polynomial parameter radius and parameter sensitivity over the entire class.
Consequently, using the bounds $B_{\mathrm{lat},n}$ and $\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}$ from \cref{prop:smooth-latent-complexity}, evaluated at the choices above,
\begin{align}
 \logN_{\mathrm{cov}}(u,{\mathcal{S}}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}},d_{\mathrm{bal},\infty})
 &\le\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}
 \log\left(1+2B_{\mathrm{lat},n}\mathsf{Lip}_{\mathrm{lat},n}^{\mathrm{par}}/u\right),\nonumber\\
 \Gamma_{n,\varepsilon,\delta}
 &\lesssim\mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{lat}}\log(en/\varepsilon)
       +\log(2/\delta).
 \label{eq:accuracy-cover-bound}
\end{align}
This establishes the approximation and complexity claims used in \cref{thm:main-generation-error-formal}.
The exact empirical minimizer exists measurably by the compactness and continuity argument in \cref{prop:smooth-latent-complexity}; the theorem permits any measurable approximate minimizer with the specified scaled-objective gap.

Apply \cref{thm:balanced-score-oracle} with its predictor radius $R_{\mathrm{pred}}=R_{\mathrm{out},n}$, the cover radius in \cref{eq:accuracy-component-tolerances}, and optimization tolerance $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}$.
With probability at least $1-\delta$, this gives \cref{eq:main-balanced-score-oracle}.
The uniform output bound and $t\ge t_0>0$ ensure that the learned score is finite-valued and Borel on the full state domain, as required by \cref{cor:randomized-grid-update-bound}.
In that corollary, $A/M\le1/2$ makes the shifted terminal-time error $\lesssim\varepsilon$ by \cref{eq:accuracy-diffusion-times}; the early-stopping error is at most $\varepsilon/4$.
Also $A\ge T-t_0$ and $M\ge2A$, so
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{A\sqrt{T-t_0}}{M}\le\frac{A}{\sqrt{M}}.
\end{equation*}
Taking square roots of \cref{eq:main-balanced-score-oracle} proves the main predictive bound.
                                                                                                                    
The task-conditional consequence is derived separately in \cref{cor:task-conditional-prompt-rate} by combining this predictive bound with \cref{eq:main-generation-error-decomposition}.
Finally $A\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon)$ and $(R_{\mathrm{out},n}+R_{\mathrm{lab},n})^2\lesssim \log(e/\varepsilon)$ give the displayed logarithmic form.
The probability statement is for each model separately, with uniformly bounded failure probability and common deterministic constants.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Let $d_z=d_\theta=1$, $\beta=\beta'=\beta_\theta=1$, with sample-only prompts and $\delta=n^{-1}$.
In this paragraph $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}$ hides powers of $\log(en)$ only.
Let $t_{0,n}$ and $T_n$ denote the choices in \cref{eq:accuracy-diffusion-times} evaluated at $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$.
With $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, the diffusion and radius choices give
\begin{equation*}
 t_{0,n}\asymp \log(en)/n,\qquad
 \sigma_{t_{0,n}}^{-1},R_{\mathrm{bal},n},R_{\mathrm{num},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
 =\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^{1/2}),\qquad J_{\theta,n}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n).
\end{equation*}
The state, final-output, and label radii, $T_n$, and $A_{t_{0,n},T_n}$ are logarithmic in $n$.
Formula \cref{eq:accuracy-component-tolerances} gives
\begin{equation*}
 \varepsilon_{\ell,n}^{-1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^2),\quad
 \varepsilon_{a,n}^{-1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n),\quad
 \varepsilon_{b,n}^{-1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^{1/2}),\quad
 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{rat},n}^{-1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^{1/2}).
\end{equation*}
There are ${\mathcal{O}}(\log n)$ overlapping time patches.
On patches with $a_j\le1$, $h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}\le7a_j$ and $\sigma_{a_j}^2\asymp a_j$; on later patches, $\sigma_{a_j}^{-1}={\mathcal{O}}(1)$ and $h_j^{\mathrm{loc}}\le T_n$.
Since $H_{q,x}=0$, \cref{eq:smooth-latent-local-denominator-modulus,eq:smooth-latent-local-numerator-modulus} give
\begin{equation*}
 \max_j H_{a,j,n}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n),\qquad
 \max_j H_{b,j,n}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^{3/2}).
\end{equation*}
The two-dimensional state--time basis size is therefore
\begin{equation*}
 \mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{basis},n}^{\mathrm{lat}}
 =\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}\!\left(n\{(n\,n)^2+(n^{3/2}n^{1/2})^2\}\right)
 =\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^5).
\end{equation*}
The likelihood and fixed ratio module sizes are $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^3)$ and $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^2)$, respectively.
The token width is ${\mathcal{O}}(J_{\theta,n})$; the attention projection count is $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^2)$ and its forward cost is $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}((n+J_{\theta,n})J_{\theta,n}^2)=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^3)$.
Repeated likelihood evaluation costs $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^4)$.
Thus the basis term dominates the numbers of trainable parameters, stored coefficients, and forward-pass operations, each bounded by $\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^5)$.
Substitution into \cref{eq:main-predictive-sufficient-choices} with $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ gives $N=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n^6)$ and $M=\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}(n)$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:main-task-uncertainty-and-minimax-rate}
\emph{Uniform upper bound.}
For every $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ and $n\ge1$, the following bound holds with an implicit constant depending only on the common class parameters:
\begin{equation}
 {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathrm{KL}\!\bigl(
 P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\,\|\,P(\cdot\mid H_n)\bigr)
 \lesssim\frac{\log(en)}{n}.
 \label{eq:main-predictive-information-bound}
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(P(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)
 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}
\end{equation*}
uniformly over $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$.

\emph{Matching lower bound.}
If $H_\theta>0$, $\underline{p}<1$, and $L_\beta>1$, then, for all sufficiently large $n$,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}&\lesssim\sup_{\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}}\mathcal{R}_n^{\mathrm{or}}(\mathcal{M})\\
 &\le\sup_{\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}}
 {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(P(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)
 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:main-task-uncertainty-bound}
\end{equation}
The lower bound allows any number of independent training episodes, including a countably infinite sequence, and unrestricted computation at generation time.
The implicit constants and the sufficiently-large-$n$ threshold depend only on the common class parameters.
The worst-case model and prior may depend on $n$, while all class parameters remain fixed.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:posterior-averaging}
Suppose the posterior-mixture identity in \cref{eq:main-posterior-predictive-mixture} holds, and let $h \mapsto Q(\cdot \,|\, h)$ be any measurable probability kernel from ${\mathcal{H}}_n$ to ${\mathcal{Z}}$.
Then
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathsf{TV}}\!\left(P(\cdot \,|\, H_n), P(\cdot \,|\, H_n, \Theta)\right)\right]
  \le
  2{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\mathsf{TV}}\!\left(Q(\cdot\mid H_n), P(\cdot \,|\, H_n, \Theta)\right)\right].
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Write $Q_h=Q(\cdot\mid h)$ and $R_{h,\theta}=P(\cdot\mid h,\theta)$.
On the common full-measure set where the posterior-mixture identity holds, convexity of TV gives
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathsf{TV}}(Q_h,P(\cdot\mid h))
 \le\int{\mathsf{TV}}(Q_h,R_{h,\theta})\Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta\mid h).
\end{equation*}
These TV distances are measurable and bounded by one: on a standard Borel space, the defining supremum can be taken over a countable generating algebra.
Taking expectations and applying the tower property yields
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}(Q(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n))
 \le{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}(Q(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)).
\end{equation*}
The triangle inequality through $Q(\cdot\mid H_n)$ proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:latent-predictive-information}
Fix $d_\theta\in\mathbb{N}$ and a probability kernel $\theta\mapsto G_\theta$ on a standard Borel observation space.
Let $\Theta$ have any Borel prior $\Pi$ on $[0,1]^{d_\theta}$, and, conditionally on $\Theta=\theta$, let $U_1,U_2,\ldots$ be i.i.d. with law $G_\theta$.
Suppose that, for some $K_{\mathrm{KL}}\in[0,\infty)$ and $\beta_\theta\in(0,1]$,
\begin{equation}
 \mathrm{KL}(G_\theta\|G_{\theta'})
 \le K_{\mathrm{KL}}\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{2\beta_\theta}
 \quad\text{for all }\theta,\theta'.
 \label{eq:latent-kl-modulus}
\end{equation}
There is a constant $C_0$, depending only on $(d_\theta,\beta_\theta,K_{\mathrm{KL}})$, such that for every integer $m\ge1$,
\begin{equation}
 I(\Theta;U_{1:m})
 \le C_0+\frac{d_\theta}{2\beta_\theta}\log m.
 \label{eq:latent-cumulative-information}
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every $n\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 {\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{KL}\!\bigl(
 G_\Theta\,\|\,\mathsf{Law}(U_{n+1}\mid U_{1:n})\bigr)
 \le
 \frac{C_0+\frac{d_\theta}{2\beta_\theta}\log(n+1)}{n+1}.
 \label{eq:latent-one-step-kl}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Fix $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$.
The latent cube admits a Euclidean $\varepsilon$-net $\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_{J_\varepsilon}\}$ with $J_\varepsilon\le C_{d_\theta}\varepsilon^{-d_\theta}$.
For example, subdivide each coordinate into intervals of length at most $\varepsilon/\sqrt{d_\theta}$ and use their centers.
Define the reference law
\begin{equation*}
 Q_{m,\varepsilon}:=
 J_\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J_\varepsilon}G_{\theta_j}^{\otimes m}.
\end{equation*}
For each $\theta$, choose a nearest net point.
The mixture dominates $J_\varepsilon^{-1}G_{\theta_j}^{\otimes m}$, so product additivity of KL and \cref{eq:latent-kl-modulus} give
\begin{equation}
 \mathrm{KL}(G_\theta^{\otimes m}\|Q_{m,\varepsilon})
 \le \log J_\varepsilon+mK_{\mathrm{KL}}\varepsilon^{2\beta_\theta}.
 \label{eq:latent-reference-mixture-bound}
\end{equation}
Let $\overline G_m:=\int G_\theta^{\otimes m}\Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta)$ be the actual marginal law of the examples.
Splitting the log density ratio through $\overline G_m$ and integrating gives
\begin{equation*}
 \int\mathrm{KL}(G_\theta^{\otimes m}\|Q_{m,\varepsilon})
       \Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta)
 =I(\Theta;U_{1:m})+
   \mathrm{KL}(\overline G_m\|Q_{m,\varepsilon}).
\end{equation*}
The nonnegative final term can be dropped.
Choose $\varepsilon=m^{-1/(2\beta_\theta)}$ in \cref{eq:latent-reference-mixture-bound}.
This proves \cref{eq:latent-cumulative-information} after absorbing $\log C_{d_\theta}+K_{\mathrm{KL}}$ into $C_0$.
The equal weights in $Q_{m,\varepsilon}$ are used only to obtain the bound; the actual prior $\Pi$ has not been replaced or restricted.

Put $\mathcal{I}_k=I(\Theta;U_{k+1}\mid U_{1:k})$.
The chain rule gives
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\mathcal{I}_k=I(\Theta;U_{1:m}).
\end{equation*}
To prove monotonicity, set $F=U_{1:k}$, $A=U_{k+1}$, and $B=U_{k+2}$.
Applying the mutual-information chain rule to $I((\Theta,A);B\mid F)$ in the two possible orders gives
\begin{equation*}
 I(\Theta;B\mid F)+I(A;B\mid\Theta,F)
 = I(A;B\mid F)+I(\Theta;B\mid A,F).
\end{equation*}
Conditional independence makes $I(A;B\mid\Theta,F)=0$.
Conditional exchangeability identifies the first term with $\mathcal{I}_k$ and the final term with $\mathcal{I}_{k+1}$.
Hence
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal{I}_k-\mathcal{I}_{k+1}=I(U_{k+1};U_{k+2}\mid U_{1:k})\ge0.
\end{equation*}
Therefore
\begin{equation*}
 (n+1)\mathcal{I}_n\le\sum_{k=0}^n\mathcal{I}_k
 =I(\Theta;U_{1:n+1}).
\end{equation*}
Finally, conditional independence gives
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal{I}_n={\mathbb{E}}\mathrm{KL}\!\bigl(
 G_\Theta\,\|\,\mathsf{Law}(U_{n+1}\mid U_{1:n})\bigr).
\end{equation*}
Combining the last two displays with \cref{eq:latent-cumulative-information} proves \cref{eq:latent-one-step-kl}.
The preceding finite bound also justifies every mutual-information chain-rule identity used here.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Fix $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$.
Let $U_i=Z_i$ in the sample-only model and $U_i=(X_i,Z_i)$ in the input--output model.
Let $G_\theta$ denote its one-example law, with density $g_\theta$ on the unit-volume cube.
Using $\log x\le x-1$ and the normalization of the two densities,
\begin{equation*}
 \mathrm{KL}(G_\theta\|G_{\theta'})
 \le\int\frac{(g_\theta-g_{\theta'})^2}{g_{\theta'}}
 \le\frac{1}{\underline{p}}
        \|g_\theta-g_{\theta'}\|_\infty^2
 \le\frac{H_\theta^2}{\underline{p}}
       \|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{2\beta_\theta}.
\end{equation*}
Thus Lemma~\ref{lem:latent-predictive-information} applies uniformly over all allowed task maps and all Borel priors.

In the sample-only model, the one-step predictive law is $P(\cdot\mid C_n)$, and the oracle is $P_\Theta$.
\Cref{eq:latent-one-step-kl} proves the asserted KL bound.

In the input--output model, put $\mathcal{I}_n=I(\Theta;U_{n+1}\mid U_{1:n})$.
Conditional independence and the KL chain rule give
\begin{align*}
 &{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathrm{KL}\!\bigl(
 P^{Z\mid X}_\Theta(\cdot\mid X_\star)
 \,\|\,P(\cdot\mid C_n,X_\star)\bigr)\\
 &\quad=I(\Theta;Z_\star\mid C_n,X_\star)\\
 &\quad\le I(\Theta;(X_\star,Z_\star)\mid C_n)=\mathcal{I}_n.
\end{align*}
The inequality follows by dropping the nonnegative term $I(\Theta;X_\star\mid C_n)$.
In particular, the argument does not require a task-independent input marginal and still conditions the predictive distribution on the query $X_\star$.
This proves \cref{eq:main-predictive-information-bound} in the second model.

Pinsker's inequality ${\mathsf{TV}}(P,Q)^2\le\mathrm{KL}(P\|Q)/2$ and Jensen's inequality yield
\begin{equation*}
 \begin{aligned}
 &{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(P(\cdot\mid H_n),P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)\\
 &\quad\le\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}
 \mathrm{KL}\!\bigl(P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)
       \,\|\,P(\cdot\mid H_n)\bigr)}
 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
For the lower bound, we construct a hypercube of smooth densities, apply an Assouad testing argument~\citep{tsybakov2009introduction}, and embed the entire family into a one-dimensional latent path.

Choose a nonzero $\psi\in C_c^\infty((0,1))$ with $\int\psi=0$ and $\|\psi\|_\infty\le1$, extended by zero outside $(0,1)$.
Write $b_1=\int_0^1|\psi|>0$ and $b_2=\int_0^1\psi^2>0$.
For sufficiently large $n$, set
\begin{equation*}
 m=\left\lfloor\tfrac{1}{4}\log_2 n\right\rfloor\ge1,
 \qquad a=\kappa\sqrt{m/n},\qquad K=2^m,
\end{equation*}
where $\kappa>0$ is a fixed small constant chosen below.
For $v\in\{-1,1\}^m$, define
\begin{equation*}
 p_v(z)=1+a\sum_{j=1}^m v_j\psi(mz_1-j+1),
 \qquad z\in[0,1]^{d_z}.
\end{equation*}
The perturbations have disjoint supports and zero integral, so $p_v$ is a density with $1-a\le p_v\le1+a$.

We check spatial regularity under \cref{eq:holder-norm-convention}, including integer $\beta$.
Recall $m_{\beta}=\lceil\beta\rceil-1$ and $\vartheta_{\beta}=\beta-m_{\beta}\in(0,1]$ from Appendix~\ref{app:subsec:regularity-conventions}, and write $\widetilde{p}_v=(p_v-1)/a$.
Disjointness gives $\|\partial_1^s \widetilde{p}_v\|_\infty\le C_s m^s$ for every fixed integer $s\ge0$; derivatives involving other coordinates vanish.
The $m_{\beta}$th derivative has both a uniform bound $Cm^{m_{\beta}}$ and a Lipschitz bound $Cm^{m_{\beta}+1}$.
Thus
\begin{equation*}
 |\partial_1^{m_{\beta}}\widetilde{p}_v(z)-\partial_1^{m_{\beta}}\widetilde{p}_v(z')|
 \lesssim\min\{m^{m_{\beta}},m^{m_{\beta}+1}\|z-z'\|_2\}
 \le Cm^\beta\|z-z'\|_2^{\vartheta_{\beta}}.
\end{equation*}
Consequently $\|p_v\|_{C^\beta}\le1+C_{\beta,d_z,\psi}a m^\beta$.
Since $a m^\beta\to0$, all these densities satisfy the fixed spatial norm bound $L_\beta>1$ and lower density bound $\underline{p}<1$ for large $n$.

Let $P_v$ have density $p_v$, let ${\mathbb{E}}_v:={\mathbb{E}}_{P_v^{\otimes n}}$, and let $d_{\mathrm{H}}$ denote Hamming distance.
Direct integration gives
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathsf{TV}}(P_v,P_w)=\frac{a b_1}{m}d_{\mathrm{H}}(v,w),
 \qquad
 \mathrm{KL}(P_v^{\otimes n}\|P_w^{\otimes n})
 \le\frac{8na^2b_2}{m}=8\kappa^2b_2
 \quad\text{if }d_{\mathrm{H}}(v,w)=1.
\end{equation*}
For the KL bound, take $a\le1/2$ and use $\mathrm{KL}(P_v\|P_w)\le\int(p_v-p_w)^2/p_w$ on the single differing bump.
Choose $0<\kappa\le\min\{1,(8\sqrt{b_2})^{-1}\}$.
Pinsker's inequality then gives ${\mathsf{TV}}(P_v^{\otimes n},P_w^{\otimes n})\le1/2$ for neighboring vertices.

For any Borel probability-valued estimator $Q_D$, where $D=Z_{1:n}$, let $\widehat{v}(Q_D)$ be the lexicographically first minimizer of $w\mapsto{\mathsf{TV}}(Q_D,P_w)$.
This is measurable: TV from a fixed probability measure on the cube is a supremum over a countable generating algebra.
The definition of $\widehat{v}(Q_D)$ and the triangle inequality imply
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathsf{TV}}(Q_D,P_v)\ge\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathsf{TV}}(P_{\widehat{v}(Q_D)},P_v)
 =\frac{a b_1}{2m}d_{\mathrm{H}}(\widehat{v}(Q_D),v).
\end{equation*}
For each bit, pair the vertices that differ only in that bit.
The sum of the two testing-error probabilities in each pair is at least $1-{\mathsf{TV}}(P_v^{\otimes n},P_w^{\otimes n})\ge1/2$.
Averaging uniformly over all vertices therefore gives error probability at least $1/4$ for each bit.
Summing over the $m$ bits yields
\begin{equation*}
 2^{-m}\sum_v{\mathbb{E}}_v{\mathsf{TV}}(Q_D,P_v)
 \ge\frac{a b_1}{8}\gtrsim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}},
\end{equation*}
because $m\asymp\log(en)$.
The estimator need not have a density.
Randomized probability-valued estimators satisfy the same bound by averaging their output measures over the auxiliary randomization and using convexity of TV.

We next embed all $K=2^m$ densities into the fixed latent class.
Enumerate the vertices as $v^{(1)},\ldots,v^{(K)}$, put $\lambda_j=(j-1)/(K-1)$, and let $\bar p_\lambda$ interpolate linearly between $p_{v^{(j)}}$ and $p_{v^{(j+1)}}$ on each interval $[\lambda_j,\lambda_{j+1}]$.
Convexity preserves normalization, positivity, and the spatial norm.
Since $\|p_v-p_w\|_\infty\le2a$, summing the intervalwise Lipschitz bounds gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|\bar p_\lambda-\bar p_{\lambda'}\|_\infty\le2a(K-1)|\lambda-\lambda'|,
 \qquad
 2aK\le Cn^{-1/4}\sqrt{\log n}\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation*}
For sufficiently large $n$, this Lipschitz constant is at most the fixed $H_\theta>0$.
Set $g_\theta(z)=\bar p_{\theta_1}(z)$ for $\theta\in[0,1]^{d_\theta}$.
This map is jointly Borel and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 \|g_\theta-g_{\theta'}\|_\infty
 \le H_\theta|\theta_1-\theta_1'|
 \le H_\theta|\theta_1-\theta_1'|^{\beta_\theta}
 \le H_\theta\|\theta-\theta'\|_2^{\beta_\theta},
\end{equation*}
using $|\theta_1-\theta_1'|\le1$.
Choose $\Pi_n=K^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^K\delta_{(\lambda_j,0,\ldots,0)}$.
The resulting environment belongs to $\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ with all class bounds fixed, and its oracle risk is at least $c\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ by the testing bound obtained above under the uniform prior on the vertices.
Although the hypercube has $m$ bits, its embedding uses only one latent coordinate.

For input--output prompts, set $g_\theta(x,z)=\bar p_{\theta_1}(z)$ on the unit input--output cube.
The same spatial and latent regularity checks apply.
The inputs, including $X_\star$, are uniform and independent of the task and output context.
Conditional on all these inputs, any kernel $Q(\cdot\mid C_n,X_\star)$ is an estimator based on the output context to which the preceding bound applies.
Averaging over the inputs gives the same lower bound.

Finally, fix the constructed environment $\mathcal{M}_n$.
For any rule using independent training episodes, write $\widehat Q_N^{\mathcal{M}_n}(\cdot\mid \mathsf d,h)$ for its output kernel given a training-data realization $\mathsf d$ and prompt $h$, and define the Borel kernel
\begin{equation*}
 \overline{Q}_h(A)
 :=\int \widehat Q_N^{\mathcal{M}_n}(A\mid \mathsf d,h)
 \mathsf{Law}_{\mathcal{M}_n}({\mathcal{D}}_N^{\mathrm{epi}})({\mathrm{d}} \mathsf d).
\end{equation*}
Independence of training from the fresh task and prompt, together with convexity of TV, shows that $\overline{Q}$ has no greater expected loss than the original rule.
The same construction applies to a countably infinite training sequence on its standard-Borel product space.
Hence independent pretraining cannot reduce the lower bound even when the entire environment is known.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:task-conditional-prompt-rate}
For all sufficiently large $n$, fix $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$, $N\ge1$, $\delta\in(0,1)$, and an integer $M$ satisfying \cref{eq:admissible-sampling-updates}. Set $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, and use the score class, estimator, and sampler of \cref{thm:main-generation-error} with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{opt}}\le c_{\mathrm{opt}}\log(en)/n$. With probability at least $1-\delta$ over the training episodes and their diffusion augmentations,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 &{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat{P}_{0\,|\,H_n}^{(M)},P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)\\
 &\quad\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}+\log(en)\sqrt{\frac{
 \mathsf{N}_{\mathrm{param},n,\sqrt{\log(en)/n}}^{\mathrm{lat}}\log(en)
 +\log(2/\delta)}{N}}
       +\frac{\log(en)}{\sqrt{M}}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:main-smooth-latent-generation-rate}
\end{equation}
On the same event, the displayed bound also holds for ${\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat{P}_{0\,|\,H_n}^{(M)},P(\cdot\mid H_n)\bigr)$. If $N$ and $M$ satisfy \cref{eq:main-predictive-sufficient-choices} with $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, then both TV errors are $\lesssim\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ and the integrated score error in \cref{eq:main-balanced-score-oracle} is $\lesssim\log(en)/n$. The implicit constants may also depend on $c_{\mathrm{opt}}$. Under the nondegeneracy conditions of \cref{thm:main-task-uncertainty-and-minimax-rate}, take $\delta=n^{-1}$ and these sufficient choices of $N$ and $M$. Then
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}&\lesssim \mathcal{R}_{n,N}^{\mathrm{lat}}\\
 &\le \sup_{\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}}
       {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{train},\mathcal{M}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat{P}_{0\,|\,H_n}^{(M)},P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)
 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:main-latent-minimax-sandwich}
\end{equation}
The lower bound holds for every number of independent training episodes, even if the model is known. The upper bound requires the stated choices of $N$, $M$, and the optimization tolerance.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Set $\varepsilon=\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ and restrict to $n$ large enough that $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$. Then $\log(e/\varepsilon)\lesssim\log(en)$ and $\log(en/\varepsilon)\lesssim\log(en)$. Applying \cref{thm:main-generation-error} and the covering bound in \cref{eq:accuracy-cover-bound} gives the posterior-predictive part of \cref{eq:main-smooth-latent-generation-rate}; \cref{eq:main-balanced-score-oracle} gives the stated score bound. The uniform upper bound in \cref{thm:main-task-uncertainty-and-minimax-rate} is $\lesssim\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, so the triangle inequality gives the task-conditional bound on the same training event.

For the upper minimax bound, the score class chosen above and the fitting rule are common to all $\mathcal{M}\in\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$.
Under each model, the conditional task risk is $\lesssim \sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ on an event of probability at least $1-n^{-1}$ and at most one otherwise.
Hence
\begin{equation*}
 {\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{train},\mathcal{M}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{TV}}\!\bigl(\widehat{P}_{0\,|\,H_n}^{(M)},P(\cdot\mid H_n,\Theta)\bigr)
 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}+n^{-1}
 \lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(en)}{n}}.
\end{equation*}
The constants and sufficient choices are common, so taking the supremum over models preserves this bound.
The minimax infimum is no larger than the risk of this particular common rule.
Existence is ensured by the Borel exact ERM in \cref{prop:smooth-latent-complexity}; any common Borel fitting rule meeting the prescribed approximate-ERM tolerance satisfies the same upper bound.

For the lower bound, the construction in \cref{app:subsec:sharp-prompt-lower-bound} gives, for each sufficiently large $n$, one model in $\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{lat}}$ on which every probability-valued estimator has expected task risk at least $c\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, even if given the model and independent training data.
This applies to every unknown-model learning rule in \cref{eq:latent-unknown-model-risk}.
Taking its supremum risk and then the infimum over rules proves the first inequality in \cref{eq:main-latent-minimax-sandwich}.
Averaging the predictive bound in the same way gives expected posterior-predictive TV $\lesssim \sqrt{\log(en)/n}$, without a matching $\sqrt{\log(en)/n}$ lower claim for that risk.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:smooth-latent-peak-shift}
In the sample-only model, let $d_z=d_\theta=1$ and
\begin{equation*}
 g_\theta(z)=1+\tfrac{1}{4}\cos\bigl(2\pi(z-\theta)\bigr),
 \qquad (\theta,z)\in[0,1]^2.
\end{equation*}
The latent coordinate shifts the density's peak without changing its support.
Integration over one period gives normalization, and
\begin{equation*}
 \tfrac{3}{4}\le g_\theta\le\tfrac{5}{4},\qquad
 \|g_\theta\|_{C^1}\le\tfrac{5}{4}+\tfrac{\pi}{2},\qquad
 \sup_z|g_\theta(z)-g_{\theta'}(z)|\le\tfrac{\pi}{2}|\theta-\theta'|,
\end{equation*}
since both $|\partial_zg_\theta|$ and $|\partial_\theta g_\theta|$ are at most $\pi/2$.
Joint continuity and these uniform bounds verify \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} with $\beta=\beta_\theta=1$, $\underline{p}=3/4$, $L_1=5/4+\pi/2$, and $H_\theta=\pi/2$, for any Borel prior.
The prompt consists of samples from one task; its posterior predictive averages over possible peak locations.

This family also has a simple coefficient-space geometry. Expanding the shifted cosine gives
\begin{equation*}
 g_\theta(z)
 =1+\tfrac14\cos(2\pi\theta)\cos(2\pi z)
   +\tfrac14\sin(2\pi\theta)\sin(2\pi z).
\end{equation*}
Thus, the coefficient vector
$\gamma(\theta)=\tfrac14\bigl(\cos(2\pi\theta),\sin(2\pi\theta)\bigr)$
traces a circle of radius $1/4$. Given $H_n$, the posterior-predictive density is
\begin{equation*}
 \begin{aligned}
 \bar g_n(z)
 &:=\int g_\theta(z)\,\Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta\mid H_n)
 =1+\bar a_n\cos(2\pi z)+\bar b_n\sin(2\pi z),\\
 (\bar a_n,\bar b_n)
 &:=\int \gamma(\theta)\,\Pi({\mathrm{d}}\theta\mid H_n).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Consequently, $\sqrt{\bar a_n^2+\bar b_n^2}\le1/4$, with strict inequality unless the posterior distribution of $\gamma(\Theta)$ is a point mass. The task densities therefore lie on the circle, while their posterior predictive can lie in its interior.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{ex:input-output-task-family}
Let $d_x=d_z=1$, let $\psi(x,z)=(2x-1)(2z-1)$, and set
\begin{equation*}
  g_\theta(x,z)=1+\rho(2\theta_1-1)\psi(x,z),
  \qquad \theta\in[0,1]^{d_\theta},\quad (x,z)\in[0,1]^2,
  \quad 0<\rho<1.
\end{equation*}
These are jointly continuous probability densities with $g_\theta\ge1-\rho$ and a common finite spatial $C^\beta$ bound for every fixed $\beta>0$.
Moreover,
\begin{equation*}
  \sup_{x,z}|g_\theta(x,z)-g_{\theta'}(x,z)|
  \le2\rho\|\psi\|_\infty\|\theta-\theta'\|_2.
\end{equation*}
Thus, for sufficiently small $\rho$, this family satisfies \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} with density floor $1-\rho$ and $\beta_\theta=1$, for any Borel prior.
Because $\int_0^1\psi(x,z)\,{\mathrm{d}} z=0$ for every $x$, the input marginal is uniform, while the conditional output density varies with the task.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
\label{ex:smooth-latent-families}
Let $\psi\in C^\infty([0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}})$ satisfy $\int_{[0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}}\psi(u)\,{\mathrm{d}} u=0$ and $\|\psi\|_\infty\le1$, and define
\begin{equation*}
g_\theta(u)=1+\rho(2\theta_1-1)\psi(u), \qquad \theta\in[0,1]^{d_\theta}.
\end{equation*}
For sufficiently small $\rho>0$, this family satisfies \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} with $\beta_\theta=1$.
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
\label{ex:smooth-latent-multimodal-mixtures}
Let $\varphi_1,\ldots,\varphi_K$ be fixed smooth densities with separated modes, let $\Delta^{K-1}:=\{w\in[0,1]^K:\sum_{k=1}^K w_k=1\}$, and let $w:[0,1]^{d_\theta}\to\Delta^{K-1}$ be H\"older continuous. Then
\begin{equation*}
g_\theta(u)=(1-\gamma_{\mathrm{mix}})+\gamma_{\mathrm{mix}}\sum_{k=1}^K w_k(\theta)\varphi_k(u)
\end{equation*}
satisfies \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} for sufficiently small $\gamma_{\mathrm{mix}}>0$. Thus, the relative weights of the modes may vary across tasks.
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
\label{ex:smooth-latent-exponential-families}
Fix $r\in\mathbb{N}$, let $\phi:[0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}\to\mathbb{R}^r$, and let $\eta_{\mathrm{nat}}:[0,1]^{d_\theta}\to\mathbb{R}^r$. The following is a normalized exponential family:
\begin{equation*}
g_\theta(u)=\frac{\exp\{\eta_{\mathrm{nat}}(\theta)^\top\phi(u)\}}{\int_{[0,1]^{d_{\mathrm{stat}}}}\exp\{\eta_{\mathrm{nat}}(\theta)^\top\phi(v)\}\,{\mathrm{d}} v}.
\end{equation*}
It satisfies \cref{assump:smooth-latent-task} when the coordinates of $\phi$ have bounded $C^\beta$ norms, $\eta_{\mathrm{nat}}([0,1]^{d_\theta})$ is compact, and $\eta_{\mathrm{nat}}$ is $\beta_\theta$-H\"older continuous. Hence, the class includes exponential families with bounded parameters, as well as truncated Gaussian families.
<!-- end example-5 -->
